So our gratitude first goes to them, your disciples, and among them especially to your illustrious son His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, who succeeded like none before him in bringing Africans into the Vaiṣṇava world.

Preaching in the Francophone countries of West Africa has been difficult, but recently we have seen some remarkable progress. Until 2003, there was just one center in one country of the twelve French-speaking countries in the region. But after this new center opened in Lome, Togo, in 2003, we have had good preaching success, and so last year another center opened, in the Benin Republic. We pray for your mercy so that we will be able to keep this momentum going and, by your divine grace, help make many other countries not yet linked with Lord Caitanya’s mission part of the Gauḍiyā Vaiṣṇava family.

For many years Bhakti-tīrtha Swami inspired us to distribute tons of books in the region. Some of us who received his special benediction made book distribution our major priority, trying to follow in his footsteps. As he approached the end of his physical pastimes with us, he started emphasizing another set of priorities. He said:

Now we have our own temples in Nigeria and Ghana; the temples are owned by us and built by our own devotees. There is no debt, loan, or mortgage involvement in any of our properties. Only in Cote d’Ivoire and Togo are devotees still paying rent. I want us to now focus more on becoming relevant to the people that we have been preaching to over the years. Our centers should be seen as places where people can come for relief from the pangs of material existence. After we give them nice philosophy, they should see how we are giving out free food to the needy. They should be able to visit our free clinics. In Ghana the constructing of our clinic is almost complete, and we are arranging for medical equipment from the US. In Port Harcourt, some of the Indian life members go to the temple to give free medical care to the neighbors. Now we have our own schools, and our children and the neighborhood children are attending these schools. Our founder-*ācārya,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, would say, “For a ten kilometers radius from our temples all over the world, no one should suffer from hunger.” In our temples and farm communities we should produce what we need—we should grow our own food crops.

In this way he encouraged us to be responsible pioneers and missionaries who bring not only a good message to the people but also a support system that people can depend on.

Just as Your Divine Grace left your disciples with the responsibility of unfolding your vision, so Bhakti-tīrtha Swami has equally given us that mission. We are happy to inform you that by his mercy and the mercy of all the Vaiṣṇava *paramparā,* especially you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, most of his cherished dreams for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness are manifesting.

Here in Togo, for the past three years, every day we have been serving an average of two hundred plates of *prasādam* to the needy in different parts of the city of Lome. Three times a week we also have a lunch program at the university, where we serve an average of two hundred plates per week. Besides receiving the *prasādam*, the university students receive Kṛṣṇa conscious literature. We have also just opened our restaurant, which is the first and only vegetarian restaurant in Togo.

Though barely five years old in Togo, ISKCON has received much a favorable recognition from the government. Just recently ISKCON was selected to be part of a nine-member committee called the Interreligious Alliance Response Against the AIDS Epidemic. Late last year, ISKCON was also selected to participate in the National Reconciliation Committee prior to the parliamentary election.

To paraphrase the words of another of our instructors:

Full surrender, the sine qua non of pure devotion, never occurs anywhere except here and now. There is no question of “If I had such and such or if I were at such and such, then I’d be able to serve Kṛṣṇa fully.” If you don’t surrender and serve now, you never will, because the reality of existence is always *now*! A moment wasted can never be recalled. How many opportunities for doing small services have been missed on the plea of waiting for a big service, or one worthy of “my” qualifications? The road to hell is paved with good intentions. Better something small and insignificant completed and accomplished now than the greatest and most important plan only dreamt of! Doing what we can do *now* with what we have *now* wins the great reward of hearing our beloved say, “Well done, thou good and faithful servant. Enter thou into the joy of thy Lord.”

Our congregational preaching and *bhakta* training program are enlisting more members. This year, 2008, our *bhakta* training started with fifteen new members. By your special mercy we have had quite a good number of volunteers helping with the ever-increasing temple activities. But we are yet to make enough temple-resident devotees. For this we are particularly praying to you that you let your divine mercy come through. We have often heard from authorities that it takes a devotee to make another devotee. This was certainly true in the case of your empowered disciple, our *gurudeva,* His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja. Our experience has been that we preached and searched for the willing souls, and he came and convinced them to surrender. It was usually after one of his visits that those who had been trying to join found the courage to do so. Now he is no longer with us, and making new fulltime devotees had become a daunting task.

Please mold us into devotees who can make others devotees. Invest us with the qualities to be shining examples for those who want to dedicate their lives to devotional service. We pray that by this time next year we shall have more to offer in our preaching report on the occasion of your auspicious appearance. May we constantly abide by your instructions and be protected from the snares of the illusory energy, especially pride.

The servants of your servants in Lome, Togo.  
(written by Varāha Dāsa)

## London (Soho)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

All glories to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-London-īśvara!

I feel both honored and humbled to have been selected to capture the sentiments of the devotees at ISKCON London on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Being only a *bhakta* from the congregation and not having a great deal of śāstric knowledge, I hope whatever offering I make is acceptable to you based solely upon the sincerity of my heart and an earnest desire to become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Reflecting on the past year, I would say that the time when we most greatly feel your presence is during the Prabhupāda marathon at Christmastime. This is a time where we make a special effort to distribute your books and thus follow in your footsteps by imparting the knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the misguided souls in London.

It is obvious that you are directly with us in our book distribution endeavors, because how else is it possible for us to distribute your literature to so many people in central London?

As we soldiered on day after day, we would feel great inspiration by remembering your own humble book distribution efforts in the scorching heat of Delhi despite being sick and fainting in the street.

A natural side effect of the marathon is the family atmosphere that is created as we push on day after day, encouraging and supporting one another with soothing words and delicious *prasādam*. Tasting *prasādam* after a full day out on book distribution is like tasting Kṛṣṇa himself—it’s all-attractive!

Some of us might feel we have made material sacrifices to serve you, but the reciprocation we feel—a greater taste for chanting and hearing the holy names and for relishing the reading of your books—is priceless. It is in these acts that you especially remain alive for me and for others, as if you were still physically present.

Thank you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your sweet inspiration. Only through serving you and the disciplic succession can we get Kṛṣṇa. Let us never forget this as we carry on serving you and your devotees with all our heart and sincerity.

We humbly beg that we may always be allowed to serve and become true instruments of your compassion.

Your servants at ISKCON London (Soho), UK.  
(written by Bhakta Boon)

## London (south)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept the humble obeisances of your granddisciples at the dust of your divine lotus feet.  
Fortunate are we, the fallen souls, that you left Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma* at the advanced age of 69  
To come all alone to West  
To fulfill the desire of your beloved Prabhupāda  
To single-handedly spread the holy names to every town and village.  
Fortunate are we, the fallen souls, that you gave us your Bhaktivedanta purports  
To make us realize that this life is meant for self-realization, not sense enjoyment,  
To elevate us to at least the platform of being human beings,  
To give us the opportunity to become Kṛṣṇa conscious in this very life.  
Fortunate are we, the fallen souls, that you built a house in which the whole world can live,  
To enable us to provide practical devotional service to the wonderful Deities and temples,  
To allow us to go on pilgrimage to the holy places in Bhārata, such as Vṛndāvana and Māyāpur,  
To give us an opportunity to relish good *sādhu-saṅga,* Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam.*  
Fortunate are we, the fallen souls, that millions of your books are distributed throughout the world,  
To the remotest parts of this planet,  
To offer an alternative to the cats’ and dogs’ life that we led,  
To remind us of our original constitutional position.  
Fortunate are we, the fallen souls, that your wonderful disciples are continuing your transcendental work,  
To provide spiritual guidance and shelter to fallen souls likes ourselves,  
To maintain, protect, and increase your ISKCON,  
To set an example for us to follow.  
Unfortunate are we that despite all this, we, the fallen souls, want  
To still hold on to the concept of “I and mine,”  
To try to enjoy the trappings of this material world,  
To continue to waste this incredible opportunity to go back home, back to Godhead.  
Since the grandfather is kinder than the father, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that you, as our spiritual grandfather, may kindly bestow your auspicious glance upon your fallen souls in South London.

Your fallen granddisciples in South London, UK.

## Longdenville

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru-Gaurāṅga!

By your divine grace, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is expanding in this small twin island of Trinidad and Tobago.

As early as 1962, your Divine Grace was corresponding with members of one local Hindu group and had planned to come here to start your ISKCON mission. Unfortunately for Trinidad and Tobago, that was not to be, and you started your mission in New York in 1966.

Your emissaries Vaikuṇṭhanātha Dāsa and Śāradīyā Dāsī underwent great tribulations in 1971 to preach here. Later on others, such as Citsukhānanda Dāsa, reported to you the favorable response of the people here to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The BBT archives have records of your vision for Trinidad and Tobago. You described this as an “important place,” and you desired a temple like the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira in Vṛndāvana to be built here.

Most of all, you said that the non-Hindus here will also make good Vaiṣṇavas.

So we are working on that—to make ourselves and everyone else in Trinidad and Tobago, now and always, into genuine Vaiṣṇavas.

When Lord Rāmacandra was building that bridge from Rāmeśvara to Laṅkā, a tiny spider was bringing dust for the bridge. Śrī Hanumānjī was informed by ŚrīRāma that the dust was as important as the big, big mountains Hanumān was throwing into the ocean.

We may not be as progressive as other temples, but we pray that by your divine grace our spiderlike efforts will please you. We aspire to become the servants of your servants, a million times removed.

Thank you very much.

The devotees at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandira, Longdenville, Trinidad.

## Malaga

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Pabhupāda,

O savior of the entire world! Please accept our humble obeisances!

The auspicious day of your appearance is an opportunity to remember you in a special way. Although we try to remember you with the same intensity in our day-to-day activities, we generally fail due to our neophyte condition. Although we read your books and listen to your lectures, we are rarely able to realize the sublime truth of your being nondifferent from your *vāṇī,* and thus we are missing you. We miss your company, your divine presence, which eradicates ignorance with a few words and establishes the highest conclusions with astonishing ease, clarity, and simplicity.

When somebody would praise you as a “miracle man,” you dismissed such praise, saying that your only credit was that you tried to carry out the order of your spiritual master without changing anything. In the physical absence of the spiritual master, to make his instructions one’s life and soul is the symptom of the perfect disciple. We want to follow your example and thus contribute to the preservation of your legacy.

Please give us the strength to be able to continue your mission without personal motivation, without mixing in separate interests due to our lack of qualification and sincerity. When you were here in person, your devotional energy captivated the minds of everybody around you and gave them the power to accomplish wonderful things. Your devotees were ready to do anything to please you, because you always cared for them like an affectionate father. They could feel that your concern was sincere and real.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! We aspire to follow in your footsteps and develop the same good qualities that you exemplify as a true *sādhu*:

*titikṣavaḥ kāruṇikāḥ suhṛdaḥ sarva-dehinām*  
*ajāta-śatravaḥśāntāḥ sādhavaḥ sādhu-bhūṣaṇāḥ*

**“The symptoms of a** ***sādhu*** **are that he is tolerant, merciful and friendly to all living entities. He has no enemies, he is peaceful, he abides by the scriptures, and all his characteristics are sublime.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.25.21)**

Who is more tolerant and merciful than you? You tolerate our faults in our daily service to you, and due to your mercy we have the opportunity to live in your temples, transcendental places of worship that give us the opportunity to associate with your devotees, who are like mirrors reflecting your sublime qualities.

You are the real well-wisher of all living entities, because you did not hesitate to risk your life to deliver us from unbearable suffering in this material existence. Your words do not create enemies, because you always speak for the benefit of everyone. Those who are fortunate will welcome your instructions as the highest boon, even if they cut like a scalpel. You are our only means for advancing on the path back to Godhead. Only by becoming attached to you can we become liberated from conditioned life.

*prasaṅgam ajaraṁ pāśam ātmanaḥ kavayo viduḥ*  
*sa eva sādhuṣu kṛto mokṣa-dvāram apāvṛtam*

**“Every learned man knows very well that attachment for the material is the greatest entanglement of the spirit soul. But that same attachment, when applied to the self-realized devotees, opens the door of liberation.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.25.20)**

Therefore, our desire is to understand you in truth and in this way become more and more attached to you. By our performing devotional service and chanting the holy name every day, our minds and senses are becoming gradually pacified. This is giving us hope that one day we may be able to appreciate everything you have done for us. Actually, our gratitude toward you should know no limits, because you have shown us how to mold our lives in such a way as to reach perfection in this very lifetime. *Śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei se uttama-gati, je prasāde pūre sarva āśā.* Attachment to your lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires. Please, therefore, grant us one drop of your mercy and give us strength so that we may continue to fight the illusory energy and not fall victim to her lures. May we always choose the right way and not become deviated from chanting the holy name and serving your lotus feet.

The devotees at ISKCON Malaga, Spain.  
(written by Caitanya-candra Dāsa and Gaurāṅga-līlā Dāsī)

## Manokwari

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are very merciful, we are from Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Manohara Mandir in Manokwari, Indonesia. We offer our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. We pray that you will accept us as the servants of your servant. You sacrificed everything for Lord Kṛṣṇa by following the orders of your spiritual master to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, especially in the Western countries. Because of your sacrifice, we are also able to get the nectar of your mercy.

You spread the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which allows everyone to get *prema-bhakti*. You taught everyone that the easiest way to approach God is by chanting the *mahā-mantra:*

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

On this special occasion, we pray for your mercy so that we will be able to follow the orders of guru and Kṛṣṇa. We here at the Rādhā-Manohara Mandir are learning how to apply Vedic culture, with special emphasis on organic farming and cow protection.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to you that we will always be engaged in devotional service with patience and enthusiasm so that wherever we are we will always remain in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-kī*jaya*!

The devotees at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Manohara Mandira, Manokwari, Irian Jaya, Indonesia.  
(written by Durāśaya Dāsa)

## Melbourne

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to you on your divine appearance day!

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (7.5.32) Prahlāda Mahārāja says:

Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination.

The above verse is fully exemplified in your life, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You traveled the world sprinkling the dust of your lotus feet everywhere, at every step. Those who took the opportunity to smear their bodies with that dust were able to take up the path of devotional service and become Kṛṣṇa conscious. Otherwise, this would not have been possible for persons very much inclined toward materialistic life.

Rūpa Gosvāmī has stated that five kinds of devotional activities—namely residing in Mathurā, worshiping the Deity of the Lord, reciting *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, serving a devotee, and chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra*—are so potent that a small attachment for any one of these five items can arouse devotional ecstasy even in a neophyte. \[*Nectar of Devotion*, Chapter 13\]

The association of the pure devotee is particularly important, for by that process one gains the opportunity to engage in the other potent devotional activities. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave the whole world the opportunity to engage in these devotional activities, of which they were previously unaware. This was your unique gift to the world.

Furthermore, by your association the world gained attraction and attachment to such devotional activities. By your chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* dancing, feasting, discussing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* worshiping the Deity, and performing other activities, you revealed and distributed to everyone the transcendental nectar inherent within these devotional activities, just as one may open the lid to a jar of honey for a foolish person trying to lick the outside of the bottle to taste the contents.

Furthermore, it is by your association that countless aspiring devotees, having tasted the nectar of devotional service, were able to maintain and increase their pure devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. Your association gave devotees all over the world the required strength and protection they needed to traverse the path back to Vraja, a path strewn with stones and brambles. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu confirms the essential role of the Vaiṣṇava in devotional service as follows: “The root cause of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa is association with advanced devotees. Even when one’s dormant love for Kṛṣṇa awakens, association with devotees is still most essential.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 22.83)

It is by the special mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa that he sends His pure devotees from the spiritual world to give us the powerfully attractive impetus and strength needed to turn our attention away from the allurements of material life and to divert our hearts to engaging in loving devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like a powerful magnet that is irresistibly attractive. In the history of the world we do not see anyone else who could imbue the whole world with the infectious desire to chant, dance, and feast in the celebration of love for the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa. Actually, if we begin to contemplate this feat, its inconceivable nature becomes more clearly evident. Most of us struggle to attract even our own family and friends to agree with us on many issues. Factually, most people find it difficult to get more than a handful of people to accept their views wholeheartedly.

Let us consider the attractive force you manifested. For millions of births living entities have been engaged in trying to exploit the resources of material nature for sense gratification. In the midst of this scene you enter with a presence that immediately diverts the attention of many of these conditioned souls away from this immemorial material engagement and powerfully attracts their attention to the pursuit of pure love of God with unconditional surrender.

This is not a one-off fluke or due to the accident of arriving in America during the rise of the “hippie” movement. You consistently manifested the same inconceivable potency twenty-four hours a day, seven days a week, in all your travels as you circumambulated the world many times over. Some had only a moment’s association with you, and some had less, and still they were swept up in the tidal wave of love of God that you were unleashing. Some had only a moment’s association with those who had a moment’s association with you, and still they were unable to escape the net of nectar you cast.

The dust of your lotus feet is still available to all who desire those transcendental particles. As you stated in a lecture on the *Nectar of Devotion* (20 October, 1972, in Vṛndāvana, India): “This book, *Nectar of Devotion, Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* if you read regularly, try to understand, this means you are associating with Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī directly. And if you act accordingly, then you are serving \[his\] lotus feet.”

Similarly, those who regularly try to understand your teachings reading your books and hearing your lectures and pastimes associate with you directly. And if they act accordingly, then they serve your lotus feet.

The dust of your lotus feet is always available to those who follow your instructions as they are received through the chain of disciplic succession. Such association with Kṛṣṇa’s devotee stimulates ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa, as stated by Rūpa Gosvāmī in *The Nectar of Devotion* (Chapter 26): “One may sometimes become overwhelmed with joy by seeing a devotee of the Lord.” This is equally true if we just remember the form, pastimes, or instructions of a pure devotee of the highest caliber. Therefore, especially on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, we should remember you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and every day we should always find time to do the same, for this has been demonstrated to be the most effective means of stimulating ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa.

“Simply by our remembering you, our houses become instantly sanctified. And what to speak of seeing you, touching you, washing your holy feet, and offering you a seat in our home?” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.19.33)

Your servants at ISKCON Melbourne, Victoria, Australia.  
(written by Gaṅgeśvara Dāsa)

## Mexico City

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Concentrated Mercy**

Goloka is full of opulence. There Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa are superlatively beautiful and everybody is joyfully engaged in assisting in Their transcendental pastimes, and they do nothing else. The youthful couple, *yugala-kiśora,* go by the names Madana-mohana and Madana-mohana-mohiṇī, for Their beauty has no parallel and everybody is simply attracted to Them. The opulence of Goloka has no comparison, even in the Vaikuṇṭhas, which are full of transcendental jewels, opulence, and awe and reverence. And although the inhabitants of the spiritual world are oblivious of the conditioned *jīvas* in the material world, Their Lordships, being merciful, feel for these suffering souls.

“Since all engagements are based on knowledge supplied by You, the conditioned souls can execute pious activities only when You mercifully inspire them to do so. Therefore, without taking shelter at Your lotus feet one cannot surpass the influence of the material energy. Actually, we, as personified Vedic knowledge, are always engaged in Your service by helping the conditioned souls understand You.”

This prayer of the *Vedas* personified illustrates that the *Vedas* are meant for helping the conditioned souls to understand Kṛṣṇa. All the *śrutis,* or personified *Vedas,* offered glories to the Lord again and again, singing “*Jaya! Jaya!*” This indicates that the Lord is the most glorious. Of all His glories, the most important is His causeless mercy upon the conditioned souls in reclaiming them from the clutches of *māyā*. \[*Kṛṣṇa* book 87: “Prayers by the Personified *Vedas*”\]

I do not know of anybody else so merciful as your humble self, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Certainly you are the concentrated mercy of The Divine Couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, and of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, the saviors of the most fallen. Without Their combined mercy, there is no way out of this entanglement called the material world; no doubt you are the personification of that sweet mercy.

In the above passage from *Kṛṣṇa* book, Your Divine grace writes, “Of all \[Kṛṣṇa’s\] glories, the most important is His causeless mercy upon the conditioned souls in reclaiming them from the clutches of *māyā*.” There is no greater opulence of Their Lordships than Their gracious mercy upon the fallen souls, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the personification of that concentrated mercy.

You literally came from Vṛndāvana to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda by spreading Their glories in the West. Who else is as merciful and humble as you?

Your Divine Grace, being the direct manifestation of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa’s and Gaura-Nitāi’s mercy, very humbly came to canvass and to reclaim the fallen, conditioned souls back to Godhead. Many may claim to be very advanced, but there is no comparison with your mercy and compassion toward the entire universe by bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone. While some consider themselves *pakka brāhmaṇas*, pure *brāhmaṇas*, who cannot contaminate themselves with lowborn people, you mercifully underwent severe penances such as living in the Bowery in New York.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda**

The Lord does not forget the fallen souls who have forgotten Him, and Your Divine Grace descended to save them all. Because you represent the most merciful Lord Caitanya, who is *mahā-vadānyāya,* you deserve the title “Śrīla Prabhupāda.” Why then shouldn’t everyone accept it? Why do some people invent so many objections? Was this a title conferred by some overly sentimental disciples, or did Your Divine Grace earn it by performing wonderful and extraordinary acts to fulfill the desires of Lord Caitanya and those of the great *ācāryas* like Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, who predicted that a great personality would spread the holy name all over the world? Or like Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda, who also wanted that holy name to be spread everywhere, and to whom you give all credit? This is the challenge to detractors who will not call you Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura comments in his *Anubhāṣya* on *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya* 7.11

“Without being empowered by the direct potency of Lord Kṛṣṇa to fulfill His desire and without being specifically favored by the Lord, no human being can become the spiritual master of the whole world. . . . Only an empowered personality can distribute the holy name of the Lord and enjoin all fallen souls to worship Kṛṣṇa. By distributing the holy name of the Lord, he cleanses the hearts of the most fallen people; therefore he extinguishes the blazing fire of the material world. Not only that, he broadcasts the shining brightness of Kṛṣṇa’s effulgence throughout the world. Such an *ācārya,* or spiritual master, should be considered nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa—that is, he should be considered the incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s potency. Such a personality is *kṛṣṇāliṅgita-vigraha*—that is, he is always embraced by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Such a person is above the considerations of the *varṇāśrama* institution. He is the *guru,* or spiritual master, for the entire world, a devotee on the topmost platform, the *mahā-bhāgavata* stage, and a *paramahaṁsa-ṭhākura,* a spiritual form only fit to be addressed as *paramahaṁsa* or *ṭhākura*.”

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

**Gaura-Nitāi**

You established the worship of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi in all your temples, for the purification process of the age, the *yuga-dharma,* is worshiping Them by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. This is the way to approach *mādhurya-rasa* and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. This is confirmed by our great *ācārya* Śrīla Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura in his *Manaḥ-śikṣā*:

*nitāi-pada-kamala, koṭi-candra-suśītala*  
*ye chāyāya jagata juḍāya*  
*heno nitāi bine bhāi, rādhā-kṛṣṇa pāite nāi,*  
*dṛḍha kori’ dharo nitāir pāy*

What will be the result of accepting the shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda? He says, heno *nitāi bine bhāi:* unless one takes shelter under the shade of the lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda*, rādhā-kṛṣṇa pāite nāi*—it will be very difficult for him to approach Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. *The aim of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is to enable us to approach Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and associate with the Supreme Lord in His sublime pleasure dance.* Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura advises that if one actually wants to enter into the dancing party of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, he must accept the shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda. \[Śrīla Prabhupāda’s purport; emphasis added\]

Dear brothers, don’t be misled, don’t be confused. Just be attached to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet. Don’t you chant *śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei se uttama-gati* every morning? “Attachment to his lotus feet is the highest perfection.”

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! Your concentrated mercy is showering on everyone, including me. I am always grateful to Gaura-Nitāi and my godbrothers that I met Your Divine Grace, to whom I am eternally indebted.

The devotees in Mexico City, Mexico.  
(written by Nandanandana Dāsa)

## Miami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, for you have given us everything necessary to become Kṛṣṇa conscious!

Perhaps most significantly, you have shown us by your personal example how to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One of the most significant lessons you have taught us is that there are no material impediments to becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious or to spreading this movement.

At the advanced age of sixty-nine, when most people have retired from active life, you boldly set out to cross the Atlantic Ocean on a cargo steamship, the *Jaladuta*. You came to America to fulfill the orders of your spiritual master, not with some grand arrangement but rather without men, money, or arrangements for support in America. You even suffered heart attacks on two consecutive nights during that voyage. Still you remained fixed in your determination to serve your spiritual master’s mission. In spite of many difficulties, over the following twelve years you were able to firmly establish an international spiritual society—the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Meditating on this great accomplishment, we are filled with great inspiration because of the lesson that you taught us by your personal example.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā*(9.22), Lord Kṛṣṇa says:

*ananyāś cintayanto māṁ ye janāḥ paryupāsate*  
*teṣāṁ nityābhiyuktānāṁ yoga-kṣemaṁ vahāmy aham*

**“But those who always worship Me with exclusive devotion, meditating on My transcendental form—to them I carry what they lack, and I preserve what they have.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **9.22)**

In this verse Lord Kṛṣṇa specifically promises that if we dedicate our lives to His devotional service He will supply whatever we are lacking to help us become Kṛṣṇa conscious. Of course, you were always Kṛṣṇa conscious, but we can appreciate how the Lord supplied you with everything you needed to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Superficially you appeared as an old man in ill health, with no one to assist you, but because you gave one hundred percent of your time, energy, and devotion to the service of your spiritual master and Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Lord supplied whatever you lacked to make your mission successful. You had a vision of an international society with devotees, temples, schools, farm communities, *prasādam* restaurants, and more, and Lord Kṛṣṇa worked through you to make your vision a reality. The significance of this lesson is that we also can accomplish great feats in the propagation of this movement if we fully dedicate our lives to carrying on and expanding the movement you established. The principle is that if we give everything we can give, Lord Kṛṣṇa will carry what we lack as He did for you. We should understand that Lord Kṛṣṇa is well aware of what we are able to give and what we are holding back. He will not give us everything until we give Him everything we can. As you told Dharma Dāsa (22 April 1972, Tokyo):

Kṛṣṇa does not care for how much we give to Him, but He sees how much we are keeping back for ourselves. There is the story of Kholaveca Śrīdhara, a devotee of Lord Caitanya, who although he was a very poor man, gave half of his meager income for worshiping Mother Ganges, and by so doing, he greatly pleased the Lord.

Similarly, if we give everything for Lord Kṛṣṇa, He will empower us to expand this mission unlimitedly and also become pure devotees and go back home, back to Godhead.

Sometimes we hear devotees lamenting that this movement is falling apart or that everything has already been lost since you departed from this world. Sometimes we make plans for reducing our standards of Deity worship or minimizing the standards required for living in the *āśrama,* hoping this will help us survive a temple’s shortage of devotees. Here at ISKCON Miami we feel that this approach is dangerous because if we justify reducing our standards on the basis that it is Kali-yuga and we cannot expect too much participation from the devotee community, what will we do as Kali-yuga advances more and more? Will our standards of Deity worship and our standards of *āśrama* life continue to diminish or be watered down? The result of this would be that the clear distinction between the superior standards that you established within ISKCON and the standard of life of the people in general would become obscure. Once a well-wisher advised you to adopt Western dress and eating habits if you ever hoped to have many followers, but you did not accept that advice because you were not looking for followers or anything else from the Western society. Your mission was to give people what they had forgotten, Kṛṣṇa. Similarly, we must maintain the highest standards within our temples, devotee households, and *sannyāsa āśrama,* or else the people in general will have no role model to follow. Even if they are unable to follow strictly, it is our duty to establish and maintain the highest standards, both for our own purification and as an example for the people in general.

Here at ISKCON Miami, we also have a vision. Our vision is that Coconut Grove will become a Kṛṣṇa conscious city, with *gurukula* schools, *varṇāśrama* college, *prasādam* restaurants, thousands of devotees engaged in devotional service, and everything centered on serving Lord Kṛṣṇa. We envision a society that will serve as a shining beacon for the whole world, showing by example how a city, just by being truly God centered, can simultaneously perform all of the functions of a perfectly managed city and at the same time provide an atmosphere conducive to developing Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Our vision is to inspire more and more devotees in the congregation to come to the brahminical standard to assist in the Deity program. Our mood is not to reduce our standards but to increase them. Our plan is to develop an active training program to teach devotees everything needed for Deity worship, from cooking to *pūjā*, from brahminical standards to the performance of *saṁskāras.* We want to train devotees to do everything necessary to become Kṛṣṇa conscious and to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We want to establish a real *varṇāśrama* society to fulfill your desire and your order to the devotees of ISKCON. In a Kṛṣṇa conscious city, *varṇāśrama* would be essential.

We can understand that if we faithfully and intelligently work to fulfill your mission Lord Kṛṣṇa will bless us with everything necessary for success. Although we have struggled for many years and have several times been faced with the risk of losing the temple, we have seen that Lord Kṛṣṇa has always protected us. This is proof positive that He has some important purpose for us to fulfill here in Coconut Grove. We also remember that in 1970 it was you who instructed some of your *sannyāsī* disciples to establish a temple in Coconut Grove. Therefore we feel certain of success if we continue to dedicate our lives to your mission without reservation.

Over the past several months we have experienced a very nice change of energy around the temple, and we feel very positive that Lord Kṛṣṇa is blessing us by reciprocating with our sincere attempts to maintain your mission. We fully expect that in the very near future we will see wonderful changes come about within our local devotional community.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we sincerely pray that you will continue to bless us with the inspiration and dedication to carry on your mission.

Your humble servants at ISKCON Miami, Florida, USA.

## Mumbai (Chowpatty)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet !

On behalf of all the devotees of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Temple, ISKCON Chowpatty, Mumbai, I have been requested to prepare our offering for this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā book.

The *Mahābhārata* contains an excellent exchange of questions and answers between an invisible Yakṣa (who was really Yamarāja) and Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja. The last and most important question the Yakṣa asks is “What is the greatest wonder of the world?” Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja replies, “Every day men see all kinds of creatures departing to Yama’s abode, and yet those who remain seek to live forever. This verily is the greatest wonder.”

Yet the *Mahābhārata,* though it contains the *Bhagavad-gītā,* does not really supply a practical solution for all mankind in the present age. It is only in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that the detailed method of deliverance is explained. However, it was only you who could, on the strength of your guru’s blessings and instructions, really demonstrate this method of deliverance, by which all mankind, for all time to come, could solve the most difficult problem, i.e., repeated birth, old age, disease, and death.

You demonstrated the solution of this grave problem and established this wonderful society, ISKCON, to propagate the teachings and practical, blissful, and sublime method of joyously chanting the *mahā-mantra—*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—both on beads (a minimum of sixteen rounds per day) and congregationally, anywhere, everywhere, and anytime. In this way you taught the ultimate solution to the most fundamental problem of everyone.

Thus you freely distributed transcendental knowledge, love of God, *prasādam,* practical advice on simple living and high thinking, and the method of preaching. When devotees follow your example and teachings, Lord Kṛṣṇa recognizes these preachers and arranges for their further progress back to Godhead.

We are all eternally very, very grateful to you for having so painstakingly helped millions of men and women all over the world, in over six hundred centers in over one hundred countries. May we serve you eternally by helping you successfully implement your transcendental program.

Your grandchildren at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Temple, Chowpatty, Mumbai, India.

## Mumbai (Juhu)

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your dear Heaven on Earth project at Juhu, dedicated to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsabihārījī, has considerably increased the preaching potential. We have formed an Integrated Preaching Program to bring together all the preaching departments, namely the Nāma-haṭṭa programs, the youth forum, and the ladies’ *sat-saṅga* programs. Under this umbrella program we have implemented the Bhakti-vṛkṣa program, which is successfully inspiring many aspiring devotees to advance rapidly in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have also introduced a monthly three-session *Bhagavad-gītā* course to bring in newcomers. And our monthly Sunday program, Mahā Prīti-Vardhanam, brings together all the congregational devotees. In collaboration with the Māyāpur Institute of Higher Education and Training (MIHET), we are also planning educational programs for our devotees. All these cultural and educational programs have been enhanced by the facilities provided by the Bhaktivedanta Cultural and Educational Complex, which is part of the Heaven on Earth project.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, at first the devotees advised you not to move to Juhu, but you strongly decided to make it a home for your beloved Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsabihārījī. You told the devotees that once a temple is built, thousands of people will flock here. Your words have proven true. Thousands and thousands of people are flocking to your ISKCON Juhu every day. Therefore we have started the campus preaching program, to give an opportunity to these visitors to get a stronger taste of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. With the increased number of visitors, our book distribution has also increased. In addition, we are distributing books on the Internet under the “e-distribution” program.

The land we have acquired in Sastri Nagar has been almost cleared of all governmental formalities. We are now ready to start constructing a new building for the Bhaktivedanta Swami Mission School. We will then shift the present school from the Juhu premises to Sastri Nagar. This will enable us to have more space on Hare Krishna Land for an improved *brahmacārī āśrama*, a *sannyāsī āśrama*, a *mātājī āśrama*, a devotee guest *āśrama,* a dormitory, administrative offices, and so on. Please guide us to do this project properly.

During Govardhana Pūjā in 2007, we humbly invited Śrī Girirāja-śilā to accept worship at your ISKCON Juhu temple. He has mercifully accepted our invitation, and He is now happily adorning the altar of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsabihārījī. The Dvārakā-śilā has also come to us, and we have placed Him on the altar of Śrī ŚrīSītā-Rāma-Lakṣmaṇa-Hanumān.

Due to the proximity of the sea, the *kalaśas* on top of the temple domes have tarnished. We are planning to replace them with gilded ones. We will use *kalaśas* made of copper and place a beautifully designed flag with the symbol of Garuḍa on it, under the guidance of His Holiness Girirāja Swami Mahārāja.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wished that ISKCON Juhu become a world-class preaching center. Please give us the intelligence to understand your mood and make us an instrument to fulfill your transcendental desire.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us this rare opportunity to render service at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsabihārījī.

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Juhu, Mumbai, India.

## Mumbai (Mira Road)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you would sometimes quote *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.19.21 (*mad-bhakta-pūjābhyadhikā*) to indicate that worshiping Kṛṣṇa’s devotees is better than worshiping Him directly.

In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 11.28), Lord Caitanya quotes the *Ādi Purāṇa* as follows:

*ye me bhakta-janāḥ pārtha na me bhaktāś ca te janāḥ*  
*mad-bhaktānāṁ ca ye bhaktās te me bhakta-tamā matāḥ*

**“\[Lord Kṛṣṇa told Arjuna:\] ‘Those who are My direct devotees are actually not My devotees, but those who are the devotees of My servant are factually My devotees.’”**

In the *Caitanya-Bhāgavata* (*Ādi* 1.8), Śrīla Vṛndāvana Dāsa Ṭhākura has written:

*“āmāra bhaktera pūjāāmā haite baḍa”*  
*sei prabhu vede-bhāgavate kailā daḍha*

**“That same Supreme Personality of Godhead \[Lord Caitanya\] has declared in the** ***Vedas*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****, ‘Worship of My devotees is superior to worship of Me.’”**

Thus on this most auspicious occasion, we followers of Your Divine Grace wish to glorify you to the highest extent of our frail human capacity. Although such praise of you is never enough, never fully descriptive of the glorious truth of your personality, we still beg you to please accept this effort to honor you on this holiest day of your divine appearance.

Some qualities from the ocean of your good qualities:

S – Special  
R – Rare  
I – Intelligent  
L – Loving  
A – Adoring  
P – Pure  
R – Radiating  
A – Ambitious for Kṛṣṇa  
B – Bold  
H – Hard-working  
U – Untiring  
P – Peaceful  
A – Aiming high for Kṛṣṇa  
D – Daring  
A – Attractive

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for fulfilling the order of your Guru Mahārāja to preach the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu in English throughout the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for crossing the Atlantic at an advanced age on the *Jaladuta,* undergoing so many hardships. On the ship you wrote beautiful verses to your dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, asking to be a puppet in His hands, to dance at His command. Please shower your mercy so that even we can recite these beautiful verses and thus fully surrender to the Lord’s will.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for living forever in your books. Your books are supremely powerful and are nondifferent from you. They are able to deliver all the fallen souls for the next ten thousand years. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You said that your books were so powerful that they themselves could go out to distribute themselves, so it is only due to your causeless mercy that you have given us fallen souls a chance to distribute your divine books and get your blessings. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give your mercy so that we, your servants, can distribute your books very joyfully to everyone during the Gītā Marathon, as well as throughout the year.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as mentioned above, while on the *Jaladuta* you wrote wonderful prayers, in which you stated:

*āniyācho jadi prabhu āmāre nācāte*  
*nācāo nācāo prabhu nācāo se-mate*  
*kāṣṭhera puttali jathā nācāo se-mate*

**“O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.”**

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has written:

*yad api samādhiṣu vidhir api paśyati na tava nakhāgra-marīcim*  
*idam icchāmi niśamya tavācyuta tad api kṛpādbhuta-vīcim*

**“Although Brahmā in full** ***samādhi*** **cannot catch a glimpse of even a ray of the effulgence from the tips of Your toenails, still, O Acyuta, because I have heard of the waves of Your astonishing mercy, I long to receive Your grace.”**

*bhaktir udañcati yadyapi mādhava na tvayi mama tila-mātrī*  
*parameśvaratā tad api tavādhika-durghaṭa-ghaṭana-vidhātrī*

**“O Mādhava! Although I don’t possess even a sesame seed of** ***bhakti*** **for You, still, by Your inconceivable power, which makes the impossible possible, please fulfill the desires of my heart.”**

Similarly, in our present conditioned state of life we feel it is impossible for us to completely surrender and develop pure devotional service. But we are sure that by your divine glance and transcendental mercy we can achieve the impossible, i.e., *ahaitukī-bhakti*.

The following are the reports for the last year:

New temple for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī on Mira Road: All the major hurdles are behind us, and things are at the final stages to get the plans approved. His Grace Jagjīvan Prabhu and Harsha Govind Prabhu are working hard on the legal front so that the temple construction can start. A lot of devotees are very enthusiastic about the construction of the new temple. Currently we are working on finding a suitable architect.

New temple for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana in Kharghar: A few of our senior *brahmacārīs* have been transferred to the Khargar project. His Grace Sura Dāsa Prabhu, who worked hard to set up the management systems at Mira Road, has been appointed as the president of the Khargar project.

Preaching center at Bhavanagar: A few congregational devotees, headed by His Grace Kundal Kṛṣṇa Prabhu, along with a couple of *brahmacārīs*, have opened a preaching center at Bhavanagar and put on many successful programs in a short period of time. Devotees are searching for suitable land in Bhavanagar to build a temple.

Preaching at Vapi: His Grace Kamal Lochan Dāsa and other congregational members set up a nice ten-day Hare Kṛṣṇa festival in December and also distributed many books. Inspired by the programs, a few people have come forward to donate land, and we hope things will be finalized in a few months

Preaching in Thane: A three-day Hare Kṛṣṇa festival was organized in Thane in April, and many educated people were introduced to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Now regular programs are going on there.

Life membership program: A team of twenty *brahmacārīs* headed by His Grace Chand Copi Dāsa brings in practically all the funds to maintain the Mira Road temple, and we pray you will empower to bring in enough funds to build the new temple also.

Bhaktivedanta College: By the inspiration of His Holiness Gopāl Krishna Goswami we have started *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā* overview, Bhakti Sadācāra, and Bhakti-śāstrī courses in both Hindi and English. A lot of new teachers are being trained, and in a couple of years we wish to have full-fledged classrooms and other facilities completely dedicated to the college. We also want to make our temple education-oriented, keeping the college as the center.

The Narasiṁha Institute for Children’s Education (’’NICE’): With the sincere efforts of Her Grace Bhāgavatī-kṛpāMātājī and Her Grace KṣīrodakaśāyaṇīMātājī, many children are adopting the Kṛṣṇa conscious way of life. Now the NICE programs are being held in Mira Road, Thane, and Kandivali.

Bhāgavata Kathā: Bhāgavata Kathās by His Holiness Rādhā-Govinda Mahārāja, His Holiness Gaura Nārāyaṇa Mahārāja, and His Grace Pṛthu Dāsa are all well-attended, and ISKCON Mira Road hosts the largest Bhāgavata Kathā, by His Holiness Rādhā-Govinda Mahārāja.

Youth programs: Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are really lacking zeal in this area. We promise you that we will do something in this regard this year.

Other preaching programs: His Grace Lalgovind Dāsa and a group of boys are sincerely developing preaching programs between Malad and Virar.

So we all are trying to fulfill your instructions, but we are only able to do so to some limited extent, which is also possible only by your causeless mercy. We are doing only a fraction of our potential; we can improve in multifarious ways the quantity and quality of our activities and dealings. Please shower your causeless mercy on ISKCON Mira Road so that we can fulfill all the instructions you have given us. You have instructed all your followers to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, unlimitedly. But we are very limited, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and so we very much need your mercy to make any progress in this regard.

Please forgive us for any mistakes, offenses, neglect, or oversight we may have consciously or unconsciously committed during this and previous years. Please have mercy on us and all the loyal devotees here. We pray that we can serve you selflessly, with deep dedication, and never be separated from the shelter of your lotus feet. Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly shower your mercy upon all your loyal ISKCON followers who are dedicated to expanding your glories and carrying out your instructions.

Your insignificant servants at the Mira Road ISKCON temple in Mumbai, India.

## Nagpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*aho prabhupāda dhanyo ’yaṁ yat-kīrtiṁśārṅgadhanvanaḥ*  
*gāyan mādyann idaṁ tantryā ramayaty āturaṁ jagat*

**All glory and success to Śrīla Prabhupāda because he glorifies the activities of the Personality of Godhead, and so doing he himself takes pleasure and also enlivens all the distressed souls of the universe.**

This is an extremely auspicious and pleasing occasion for everyone because you appeared on this day. Without the presence of such a *mahātmā* as yourself, the entire world would be bereft of auspiciousness. It is said in all authentic revealed scriptures that actual auspiciousness begins for living entities when they come in touch with a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Your magnanimous contribution of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the world serves as the best example for all the preachers of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

It is our great fortune that we have the opportunity to be part of a mission being led by a true, sincere, and pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa like you. It is only because of you that we have taken to the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is because of your compassion and mercy that the people of the world are becoming lovers of Kṛṣṇa today.

The real glorification of a pure devotee is to render services and follow his instructions. Therefore, on this auspicious day of your divine appearance we would humbly like to take the opportunity to submit our insignificant services for 2007–2008 at your lotus feet. And also, we fall at your lotus feet and beg you to empower us to please you by fulfilling your mission here in Nagpur.

In 2007, many unprecedented developments occurred in the Nagpur *yātrā.* For your pleasure, here are a few of them:

Śrī Jagannātha Ratha-yātrā was a great success. In the words of His Holiness Lokanāth Mahārāja, “This Ratha-yātrā was more successful than ever before.” The entire congregation, including many youths, was actively engaged.

Our ISKCON land papers also got clearance from the government, and we have begun constructing a boundary wall around our recently-acquired 3½ acres of land. Planning for the new temple is going on.

For the first time in the history of ISKCON Nagpur we distributed more than 4,000 *Bhagavad-gītās* during the Prabhupāda Marathon.

In the beginning of 2008, Lokanāth Swami performed ceremony inaugurating a public bus stop right outside our temple called “ISKCON.”

Our temple attendance increased more and more throughout the year. Congregational devotees are enthusiastically taking part in celebrating festivals.

Nagpur is getting more association of senior devotees, your disciples.

Youth preaching is also taking a new shape here. Regular youth preaching is now going on in a few colleges. In one college, the principal herself started chanting and encouraged all the students with the following words: “Personally, I have been benefited by this chanting. And I want that all of my students should chant this Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* daily.”

Congregational preaching is happening now more systematically. We have started a Vaiṣṇava Training Academy to educate, train, and counsel full-time devotees, youths, and the congregation. We have completed three sessions. Many devotees who regularly attend these classes share the testimony given by one student: “This education program has given a real direction to my Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Now I am more steady, focused, and absorbed in my chanting, and I feel enthused to preach and render services.” Many such devotees have begun chanting sixteen rounds daily, regularly reading your books, and actively rendering services in the temple. Very soon we will start a trainee preacher forum to make qualified preachers of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Nagpur.

The standards of the temple morning program have increased. Very soon we will have a library of all your books and lectures, both in physical form and digital.

This year we plan to inspire at least 108 youths to start chanting seriously and to create at least 50 ideal Kṛṣṇa conscious *gṛhastha* families committed to your mission.

As Kṛṣṇa is *ananta,* unlimited, our plans to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness are also *ananta*. But *kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana:* one cannot spread the glories of the holy name of the Lord without being specifically empowered by Him. And that empowerment comes in the *paramparā* through you.

On this day we fall at your lotus feet and beg for the purity required to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in a systematic way, for the ability to expand book distribution, for the strength to bring real unity, friendship, and love among all the devotees, and for the skill to train more and more dedicated devotees.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please make us dance, make us dance, make us dance as you like.

Thank you very much for engaging us. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your aspiring grandsons and granddaughters at ISKCON Nagpur, Maharashtra, India.  
(written by Anantaśeṣa Dāsa)

## Natal

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances at your lotus feet! All glories to you!

We cannot truly understand even a fraction of your transcendental qualities, yet we do understand that we have been blessed and caught by the waves of the *mahā-mantra kīrtana:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. These powerful waves reach our minds and hearts, creating chaos and destroying our dreams and desires for leading a life devoid of God, Lord Kṛṣṇa. The marvelous effect of this matchless gift, the holy names, is gradually spreading and conquering everyone’s minds, little by little and at every instant.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although we do not feel qualified to make such a request, we beg you not to give up on us insignificant servants. Please engage us in your service.

Your unworthy servants in Natal, Brazil.

## New Badarikāśrama

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your appearance! Please excuse my offenses in this feeble attempt to glorify those lotus feet.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.25.20) Lord Kapiladeva says to His mother Devahūti:

*prasaṅgam ajaraṁ pāśam ātmanaḥ kavayo viduḥ*  
*sa eva sādhuṣu kṛto mokṣa-dvāram apāvṛtam*

**“Every learned man knows very well that attachment for the material is the greatest entanglement of the spirit soul. But that same attachment, when applied to the self-realized devotees, opens the door of liberation.”**

There have been a few moments of clarity in my life, Śrīla Prabhupāda, when I have understood how great is this gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you sacrificed your life to give us. My life has been nothing but entanglement and attachment for this miserable body. My lack of sincerity is evident by my inability to associate with and serve your devotees properly. I put on a show, but my heart is so crusty with impious activity that there is no honesty. To preach and to distribute your books are the activities most dear to you, but I always create some excuse to avoid them and look for something to rationalize my miserly mentality. Thus, what chance is there of my becoming attached to you?

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.31.9) the *gopīs* sing:

*tava kathāmṛtaṁ tapta-jīvanaṁ*  
*kavibhir īḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham*  
*śravaṇa-maṅgalaṁśrīmad ātataṁ*  
*bhuvi gṛṇanti ye bhūri-dā janāḥ*

**“The nectar of Your words and the descriptions of Your activities are the life and soul of those suffering in this material world. These narrations, transmitted by learned sages, eradicate one’s sinful reactions and bestow good fortune upon whoever hears them. These narrations are broadcast all over the world and are filled with spiritual power. Certainly those who spread the message of Godhead are most munificent.”**

This verse perfectly applies to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because the nectar of your words describing the Lord are relieving the suffering of this fallen world. For unqualified souls like us, what greater fortune can there be than to have the opportunity to perform devotional service? Certainly you are the most munificent, having spread this mercy throughout the whole world. Because of your intense devotion and purity, you motivated even the most stonehearted persons to take up the service of Lord Caitanya without expecting anything in return.

Although these years of your physical absence have been extremely difficult, remembrance of your beautiful smile and gentle movements have been our comforts. Things are going on only by your will and the mercy of your dear Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara. What you had to endure pains our hearts and makes our troubles seem so tiny, yet you made everyone feel significant. Like a father who tolerates and shelters a nonsensical child who repeatedly exercises bad judgment, you mercifully continue to shelter us from the fire of material existence despite our offenses.

But does one use this as an excuse to continue in ignorance? No. The father also chastises the child to grab his attention. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, chastise me as you see fit so that I may come out of this ignorance and into the light of devotional service, so that I may not waste time but use what little I have left in the most urgent work of *saṅkīrtana*.

Should the child become indignant at the chastisement of the father? No. This will only increase his suffering. My suffering, Śrīla Prabhupāda, has increased only because of my failure to carefully execute your clear instructions due to my dull-headedness. Instead of being intelligent and recognizing how this body is failing, I continue to think that some material adjustment will be the cure. But the real medicine is to take shelter of you and have implicit faith in your instructions. By your example you’ve shown us how to surrender and be the Lord’s perfect instrument.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, be kind to me so that I can be your instrument and assist you in some way in Lord Caitanya’s movement. Just as He is your life and soul, you are our life and soul.

Your servants at New Badarikāśrama, Denver, Colorado, USA.  
(written by Naikātmā Dāsa)

## New Biharvana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you! And all glories to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Baṅkebihārī, who kindly came to Boise twenty years ago at your request!

This last year we have been holding three Bhakti Vṛkṣa programs. The objective is to give the participants an opportunity to cultivate their Kṛṣṇa consciousness by reading your books.

In one of the Bhakti Vṛkṣa programs, after five weeks of classes, the students were giving their realizations.

When it was Judy’s turn, she could not contain herself and said out loud: “After reading the books, I have come to the conclusion that Jesus was a Hare Kṛṣṇa. Everything he said and believed is in these books.”

Now, Judy is not a new-ager or a hippie type. Judy is a sixty-year-old devout Christian grandmother who is a little bit disillusioned by her church and trying to keep her family intact. We met her at a book table in the park and invited her to the course, entitled “Journey of Self-Discovery with Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

The group was studying your glorious earthly pastimes and your analysis of the present conditions of the world. The primary book they were reading was the condensed *Līlāmṛta, Your Ever Well-Wisher,* supplemented by *Journey of Self-Discovery,* an anthology of your talks on various practical issues of today.

After finishing *Your Ever Well-Wisher,* Bhakta Śyāma, who has been coming to the temple for several months and trying to understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness, said, “I now understand what Kṛṣṇa consciousness is. It is chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, eating *prasādam,* associating with devotees, and doing a lit bit of service for Kṛṣṇa. It is so simple!”

Such is the power of your books; such is the impact of your brief presence amongst us. The impact of associating with your pastimes is so inspiring that it changes lives, making people Kṛṣṇa conscious in a matter of weeks. This indicates that these are not ordinary people but rare souls who have taken perhaps thousands of births to come to this point. As Lord Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.19):

*bahūnāṁ janmanām ante jñānavān māṁ prapadyate*  
*vāsudevaḥ sarvam iti sa mahātmā su-durlabhaḥ*

**“After many births and deaths, he who is actually in knowledge surrenders unto Me, knowing Me to be the cause of all causes and all that is. Such a great soul is very rare.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made achieving the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa so simple. We are unable to adequately thank you. We pray to you, humbly and simply, to keep us under your lotus feet.

Your servants at New Biharvana, Boise, Idaho, USA.  
(written by Anantarūpa Dāsa)

## New Dvārakā

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept the humble obeisances of your servants at your Los Angeles ISKCON center, New Dvārakā. All glories to Your Divine Grace on the day of your all-auspicious appearance!

During your visible personal presence on earth you spent many days at your temples in Los Angeles, especially the one on Watseka Avenue. Fortunately you made many statements about Los Angeles, which we fondly recall, as they allow us to see Los Angeles through your transcendental eyes. Here are just a few of your many observations.

You once said:

I’ve seen in Los Angeles big, big palm trees, very long, but there is no fruit. In India there are palm trees like that, they bear fruit, very nice fruit, *tāl.* Very sweet fruit. So any tree which does not give us nice fruit or nice flower, that is sinful. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* lecture, London, 17 August 1973\]

Today we can see those same tall palm trees along the streets around the temple. To many they look very picturesque, but you gave us some additional insight.

The distant hills in Los Angeles inspired you to give some philosophical lessons. You said:

We see sometimes the hills from our room. Here there are many hills in Los Angeles. But they are not distinct. When you are seeing the hills from a distant place, it looks like something cloudy. But if you go still further towards the hill, you’ll distinctly find that there is something, hill. And if you come to the hill, then you’ll find so many persons are working there, so many houses are there. There are streets, motorcars, everything, all varieties. So similarly, when one wants to know the Absolute Truth by his teeny brain—“I shall make research to find out the Absolute Truth”then you’ll have vague idea, impersonal idea. And if you become a meditator, then you will find that God is situated within your heart. . . . And those who are devotees, they meet the Supreme Person face to face just like we are meeting face to face, talk face to face, serve directly. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Los Angeles, 20 April 1973\]

When we walk from the temple on Watseka Avenue out to Venice Boulevard and look southeast, we can see the same indistinct hills you saw when you were present here. And looking at those hills, we are reminded of you and the lessons you taught when talking to your followers in the temple face to face. By serving you, we will eventually come to the position of meeting the Supreme Person face to face. When we see the hills of Los Angeles, we can remember that.

Sometimes we walk on the Los Angeles beaches you walked on when you were present here. You said:

We have seen that Los Angeles on the beach, the big Pacific Ocean, but just about three yards or four yards off from the ocean we are walking. We are confident that “Although the Pacific Ocean is so big, powerful, it cannot come here.” Only a few yards off we are walking, confident. So everyone is working under the direction of the Supreme Lord. Ocean, although very powerful, very big, still, by the order of Kṛṣṇa, it cannot go beyond the limit. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, Māyāpur, 22 June 1973\]

Although in this statement you concentrated on the normal situation of the ocean staying within its bounds, you also acknowledged that Kṛṣṇa could cause the ocean to exceed its bounds. “If God wants,” you said, “this whole city of Los Angeles can be, within a second, overpowered by this water of the Pacific Ocean. Within a second. It does not take much time.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Los Angeles, 13 August 1972)

Sometimes you took a look far into the future, anticipating the big environmental crises to come at the end of the *manvantara.* You explained:

After the span of life of Cākṣuṣa Manu, there is another inundation. And then again everything comes out gradually and the water dries. Just like you see the Pacific Ocean, it is drying. Of course, we shall not live, but if hundreds years after, if somebody comes here, he will see that the Los Angeles city has expanded and the Pacific Ocean has diminished. This is going on. In this way, one day will be, there will be no Pacific Ocean. And therefore, without water, everyone will die. Simply sunshine, bright sunshine. So then again, as there is after bright sunshine there is cloud and rain, similarly, again there will be rain. Then again there will be water. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Los Angeles, 20 September 1972\]

Therefore, when we see the Pacific we can be reminded of your vision, your words, your lessons, how you saw everything in relationship to Kṛṣṇa.

Los Angeles is famously a place of incredible contrasts and wealth. On one hand there are the rich and famous living in Beverly Hills, and on the other hand you have the poor and homeless. In this regard you said, “Just like ordinarily, everyone is working in Los Angeles, hard, day and night, but somebody’s poor man, somebody’s rich man. Why? According to karma.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, Los Angeles, 20 May 1972) But even for the rich, life can be tough. You observed:

Similarly, in your country also, there are many men coming daily Los Angeles, five hundred miles by plane. This is your civilization. For two meals only, you have to make so much *tapasya*—fifty miles, hundred miles, five hundred miles, go and come back. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Japan, 22 April 1972\]

What about the LA traffic? You said:

When I was in Los Angeles, there is a freeway. So eight lines of cars running in seventy miles speed this way, and eight miles of lines running cars on the opposite side. And unfortunately one day we had one car which was running at thirty-five miles only, and our Gaurasundara was driving. \[chuckling\] Immediately he was arrested by the police. Not exactly arrested—stopped. That means you cannot run your car in this way, thirty-five miles speed. So now from impartial point of view, if we study why people are running in this way and that way . . . What is the ultimate goal? If we calculate very in cool head, the ultimate goal is sense gratification. That’s all. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Tittenhurst, UK, 13 September 1969\]

And then there is the ever-present threat of earthquakes. You apparently experienced one. You said:

I am thinking at the present moment that “I am this body.” Everyone is thinking. Therefore, when there is fear of destruction of this body, we become very much afraid, perplexed. We have seen in Los Angeles. There was earthquake, and all the neighborhood, women especially, began to scream, fearful, “Now death is coming.” So everyone is afraid. This is the material nature. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Bombay, 10 December 1974\]

In Los Angeles you began some of your most important intellectual initiatives, such as your assault on the mistaken ideas of modern materialistic science. You said:

Recently, when I was in Los Angeles, one German scientist came there. He has written one book, *Chemical Evolution*, and he has got Nobel Prize. Now he’s touring for lecturing on his theory. So in the California university there is our student, Dr. Svarūpa Dāmodara. He’s my disciple. He’s doctor in chemistry. So, when this German chemist was lecturing, theorizing that life has come from chemicals, so \[Svarūpa Dāmodara\] put the question that “Suppose if I give you these chemicals, whether you can prepare a life?” He answered in the meeting, “That I cannot say.” That means he’s not certain; still, he’s theorizing that from chemical, life has come. No, from chemical, life has not come; from life, chemical has come. This is real theory. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* lecture, Bombay, 19 February 1974)

Let us keep alive and expand your preaching mood. When we look at the Los Angeles ISKCON temple, we can see that it was once a church. You said,

In Los Angeles we have purchased that church. When it was running as a church, it was deserted. Since we have taken that, every night, every day, hundreds of people are gathering like this, because there is words of Kṛṣṇa. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, New Vṛndāvana, 6 September 1972\]

It is our duty to make sure the words of Kṛṣṇa and the names of Kṛṣṇa continue to resound in the temple and thus keep it full of people. If we slacken in this duty, it may again become deserted.

You spoke of how Kṛṣṇa was providing for the Los Angeles temple and its residents:

We have got heavy expenditure, but Kṛṣṇa is supplying. This is a fact. None of our boys and girls, they go to office or to factory or they earn. The . . . In Los Angeles, our neighborhood men, they’re very envious. They say, “How you maintain such huge establishment and you do not work?” They cannot dream that without working one can eat.” \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, Vṛndāvana, 21 October 1972\]

And today Kṛṣṇa is still supplying. Of course, today the situation is a little bit different. Some of “our boys and girls” do go to the office, or factory, or shop to work, but there is still a core of “boys and girls” (some of them getting a bit on in years) who do not go to the office or factory.

Once when you were in India and noticing that not very many people were attending your lecture you said:

This class, when it is held in Los Angeles, this full hall would have been compact. This is the difference between India and foreign countries. They are receiving this message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness very seriously. Here they are rejecting. Indians, they’re rejecting. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, Māyāpura, 14 June 1973\]

Now the situation is a little different. When we travel from Los Angeles to your temples in India, we see that the classes are full there also, and that many people in India are receiving the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness very seriously. And we know this must be immensely pleasing to you, but we hope to continue pleasing you by increasing the number of people attending lectures in Los Angeles and find other ways to increase the number of people receiving the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness very seriously.

Once you said:

If I invite all the dogs and animals of Los Angeles city to come here to hear about *Bhagavad-gītā*, it is not possible, because they are animals. But if I appeal to the human being, because he is human being, he may be interested. He should be interested, but the time is so bad that nobody is interested, but still, he may be interested. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, Los Angeles, 14 December 1973\]

Here you summarize the essence of your preaching in the modern world and the difficulties of it. Because we are following in your footsteps and are trying to make your desires our desires, your words are very meaningful to us. We should also appeal to human beings to hear the *Bhagavad-gītā* and purchase the *Bhagavad-gītā*. This is difficult. Because of the nature of Kali-yuga, many people are not interested, but because they are human beings rather than animals it is possible that some of them may become interested in hearing the *Bhagavad-gītā*. Therefore it is our duty to invite them to accept the *Bhagavad-gītā* and hear about it.

You always liked the book distribution that took place in Los Angeles. You said:

These books are very much welcome in the Western countries. I have got report just now, I have received from Los Angeles. The report is that within the three days of Christmas holidays, we have sold one *lakh* worth of books within three days. So our books are being very much welcome in the Western countries, especially the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Bombay, 12 January 1973\]

During your physical presence Los Angeles was the world headquarters of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust. You were very concerned about the quality of the publishing. You told the following story:

Recently one gentleman from India . . . He is a man of very good position, ICS, the old ICS, British ICS, Indian Civil Service. They are the topmost service.
