So he sent me one translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. So perhaps you know. Gargamuni knows. So I rejected it, that “No, this cannot be published. There are so many anomalies.” And so his daughter is here in Los Angeles. So she came with her husband and took back. So we do not publish anything which is not approved by the *ācāryas.* We are not like ordinary press, that anything and anything will come and we shall publish. No. That is not our business. It must be approved, as we are presenting *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is, no mental concoction. We don’t allow any mental concoction. It must be approved by the *ācārya,* disciplic succession. Then it is nice. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, Los Angeles, 1 July 1970\]

Today the BBT is still present in Los Angeles, and the devotees there have the same duty to insure that everything that comes out from the BBT is approved by you, the founder-*ācārya,* as well as the disciplic succession.

During the early days of the movement, you showed your appreciation for the simple efforts your young disciples made to attract people to the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You said:

In our Los Angeles temple they have made very nice puppet show, and people are appreciating very much. Even they are selling ticket at the rate of one dollar fifty cents, still, people are coming. Last Sunday I was present, and they invited, distributed pamphlets, and more than a hundred people came, and they participated with the *kīrtana* very nicely, they heard the lecture, and the function was for two hours. Still, they kept very busy themselves in eating *prasādam,* in seeing the puppet show and the cinema of Ratha-yātrā. So many things. It was very successful. And they collected about more than $150. So things have to be organized. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Hawaii, 21 March 1969\]

So, yes, things do have to be organized. Today we should not simply stick to routines. Without giving up the essence, we should always be thinking of newer and newer ways to attract people to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and contribute to the success of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Of course, one of the most attractive features of the Sunday Feast and Kṛṣṇa consciousness in general is *prasādam,* spiritual vegetarian food. You said:

The next austerity, we say that don’t take any animal food. There is no need of taking. Perhaps you are coming to our Love Feast. We can prepare. Of course, these boys and girls, they are not very expert, but still, whatever they have learned, and they are supplying *prasādam* to your American boys and girls, and people, they are appreciating very much. In Los Angeles—that is our biggest temple in your country—we get in every Love Feast day not less than two hundred guests. They come from far away with their car and they take. They like it. You see? So if you accept this austerity, that “We shall not eat meat, but we can have very nice foodstuff from grains, from fruits, from vegetables, milk, sugar, so many nice foodstuff,” you will forget. Simply you have to learn. That is not very severe austerity. Simply our process is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, how from this present consciousness we want to change to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. \[Lecture, Boston, 25 April 1969\]

Today in Los Angeles we continue to satisfy your desire that we distribute *prasādam.* We distribute *prasādam* at the Sunday Feast, at our Govinda’s restaurant during the week, and at festivals throughout the year, all for your pleasure.

So we, your disciples and granddisciples here in Los Angeles, desire to continue to please you by serving you in the temple you established here. You once said:

In Los Angeles also our program is going on very nicely. He, my disciple in charge, Karandhara—he’s present here—he’s simply doing what I instruct, and he’s doing very nicely, first class. Everyone who comes, they become enchanted with the temple, with the activities, with the disciples. So this is the way. This is called *paramparā* system. Don’t try to manufacture. \[Lecture, Detroit, 16 July 1971\]

So we pray to be able to closely follow your instructions, so that people will continue to be enchanted by the temple, the activities, and the disciples.

At times we enter your apartment in the Los Angeles temple building. It is a beautifully transcendental place, full of your quiet presence. It is touching that you would think of your apartment in LA, even when you were far away from it. Once in India you said,

Mind can run within a second many thousands of miles, immediately. Suppose I am sitting here. So I have got my apartment in Los Angeles, ten thousand miles away. I can immediately go. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* class, Ahmedabad, 13 December 1972\]

Sometimes we feel the pressures of life in a big city like Los Angeles and we want to go somewhere else. Perhaps we dream of going to the Himalayas. But you said:

You don’t require to go to Himalaya. You just remain in Los Angeles city and engage your eyes to see Kṛṣṇa, you are more than a person who has gone to Himalayas. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* class, Los Angeles, 19 December 1968\]

Or perhaps we want to go not to the Himalayas but to Vaikuṇṭha. In this regard, you said:

Just like this temple. This temple is spiritual, Vaikuṇṭha. It is not Los Angeles. Don’t think like that. It is Vaikuṇṭha. Because here every second the remembrance of Kṛṣṇa is there. Therefore it is not Los Angeles. Or anyone who remembers Kṛṣṇa always, twenty-four hours, he is living in Vaikuṇṭha. He is not in the kingdom of *māyā.* \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Los Angeles, 2 October 1972\]

Thank you for giving us the chance to always remember Kṛṣṇa and live in Vaikuṇṭha. Or perhaps we want to go to Vṛndāvana. You said:

Just like we have come here to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have no distinction between Los Angeles and Vṛndāvana. Wherever I am, I am in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, that is Vṛndāvana. That’s all. I don’t live in Los Angeles or anywhere. I live in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, therefore I live in Kṛṣṇa. So your principle should be like that. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* class, Los Angeles, 27 December 1968\]

So we shall try to make our principle like that.

Your disciples, granddisciples, and other followers in New Dvārakā, Los Angeles, California, USA.  
(written by Drutakarmā Dāsa)

## New Gokula (Millfield)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this auspicious day of your appearance!

You are the father of your disciples. Like a loving father, you are always ready to give your mercy even if your disciples and granddisciples do not deserve it. Actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are made of your mercy. We cannot claim to have any knowledge or good qualities without your transcendental mercy. If we become puffed up with knowledge and acquired qualities, then *punar mūṣiko bhava:* “Again become a mouse!” So we read your books and try to follow the example of great devotees. We follow your instructions. We practice humility as Lord Caitanya has taught and pray that you will be pleased with us in our devotional service.

Our farming community has had some problems. Now we are looking forward to a better future for the farm. By your mercy we hope to achieve the fulfillment of what an ISKCON farming community can present to the society: “Simple living and high thinking,” sustainable agriculture, organic farming, milking the cows, training the bulls, training the devotees in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, enjoying our lives in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and bathing in the ocean of your transcendental mercy.

Please bless us that we can please you in our service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda.

Your servants at ISKCON New Gokula Farm, Millfield, Australia.  
(written by Ādi Puruṣa Dāsa)

## New Gokula (Vancouver)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our Commander in Chief of the Saṅkīrtana Revolution**

Our great Lord of mercy,  
*Karuṇāvatāra* Śrī Caitanya,  
Has descended from on high  
To rescue all of us suffering souls  
Caught in Kali’s poisonous embrace.  
Our great saviors Nitāi-Gaurāṅga  
Have thus arisen together,  
Like the sun and the moon,  
To remove the darkness of ignorance  
That has stifled mankind’s hope.  
Declaring war on Kali’s dark hordes,  
Our merciful Lords have brought with Them  
Their own invincible divine *astras*  
As Their own empowered servants,  
Who alone can defeat  
Kali’s rampant tyrants and rogues.  
From among these valiant spiritual warriors,  
Śrīla Bhaktivinoda first of all  
Sounded the clarion call  
To rally all pious souls  
Under the banner of the *yuga-dharma*  
Of Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.  
His fearless son Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Ṭhākura  
Boldly traversed all of Bhāratavarṣa  
To challenge and uproot  
The vicious concoctions and shameless deviations  
Of the demon agents of Kali,  
Masquerading as pseudo-Vaiṣṇavas.  
He roared out with potent *śakti*  
The pure message of Mahāprabhu,  
That any and all souls  
Can become worthy recipients  
Of the nectarean flood of *gaura-kṛpā*  
When they simply call out with sincere hearts  
The divine names of Nitāi-Gaurāṅga  
And glorify the Divine Couple  
With the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra.*  
From among the many true servants  
Of the conquering *siṁha-guru,*  
One great commander arose  
To fulfill his great desire  
And help liberate the entire world.  
Predicted as the *senapati bhakta,*  
The commander in chief  
Of Gaurāṅga’s *saṅkīrtana* forces,  
Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda  
Humbly took up the sacred order  
Of his perfect master with unflinching faith  
And single-handedly conquered  
All of the Western, *mleccha loka.*  
Inspiring so many downcast and searching souls,  
He lovingly nurtured our budding attraction  
To follow his pure life of devotion.  
Arming us with the *kavaca* of strong *sādhana,*  
He showed us how to win the first battle  
By uprooting the demons of sin  
Still lurking in our impure hearts.  
Then he miraculously transformed  
So many into stalwart messengers  
Of the golden Lord’s gospel of love of God,  
Sending them to every town and village  
With the blessings of *nāma-saṅkīrtana—*  
The essence of the pure *gaura-vāṇī.*  
Holding tightly to the shield of his mercy,  
They continue to relentlessly push aside  
All the skeptics and cynical disbelievers  
As they spread worldwide  
The great *bhāgavata* revolution  
In the dawn of Gaurāṅga’s golden age of love.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda has armed his faithful followers  
With the invincible divine weapons  
Of the pure *harināma*  
And the *divya-jñāna*  
Of his perfect *vāṇī.*  
Under his empowered command  
His *saṅkīrtana* army of truth  
Has conquered in all directions  
To push back Kali’s entrenched dark forces.  
At last suffering mankind  
Can find new hope  
In the golden Lord’s expanding glories  
By broadcasting the greatest good news  
That we are all God’s children,  
Meant to regain His blissful service.  
And that our beautiful playful Lord  
Awaits us all to join Him again  
In His eternal festival of love,  
To be experienced even now  
In the sweet glorification  
Of His all-blissful names.  
Thus secure under our fearless commander in chief,  
The *saṅkīrtana* forces keep pushing forward,  
Following behind our ever-triumphant leader,  
Who has already cleared the path of all danger.  
We pray to always remember  
That under his enduring protection  
No harm can ever come to us,  
Neither from Kali’s relentless agents  
Nor from Māyās beguiling allurements.  
For we’re fortified by this solid conviction  
That he is all-victorious in the past,  
He is indeed victorious in the present,  
And is assuredly ever victorious in the future.  
Our commander in chief,  
*Jagad-guru* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s grateful servants in New Gokula, ISKCON Vancouver, Canada.  
(written by Gokulānanda Dāsa)

## New Goloka

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The dignity of Your Divine Grace  
Manifests like a fragrant blooming flower.  
You opened the door of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*  
With your spiritual energy and power.  
The preaching mission of Your Divine Grace  
Descends directly from Goloka *dhāma*.  
Lord Caitanya personally takes charge  
To spread the glories of Śrī Rādhā-Śyāma.  
The transcendental books by Your Divine Grace  
Are torchlights on the material path.  
Your words awake Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
To save us from worldly wrath.  
The matchless gift of Your Divine Grace:  
The chanting of the Lord’s holy name.  
Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* bestows  
All bliss, knowledge, and eternal aim.  
The causeless mercy of Your Divine Grace  
Flows in every town and village.  
*Harināma* echoes around the earth;  
The bright hope in dark Kali age.  
The instructions of Your Divine Grace  
Eternally illuminate—never get dim.  
You lighten human life by your slogan  
“Always remember Kṛṣṇa, never forget Him.”  
The shelter of Your Divine Grace—  
Cooling shade from the blazing heat.  
Protection is certain in all circumstances  
Under secure shield of your lotus feet.  
The followers of Your Divine Grace  
Always preach at every chance.  
Their devout dedication to you  
Shows by action without fake trance.

Your servants of New Goloka Dhāma, North Carolina, USA.

## New Govardhana (Murwillumbah)

**Just One Morning**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, we meditate upon your lotus feet in the following way.

While rising early in the morning, you recite the holy name of the Lord and, making your way to the bathroom for ablutions, ready yourself for serving your Guru Mahārāja. It’s 2 a.m. when you sit down and begin translating into English the greatest of all literatures, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* for the benefit of those less fortunate. They cannot comprehend the transcendental sound vibration of such a great literature, but this spotless *purāṇa* is as brilliant as the sun, and persons who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in this age of Kali will, by your mercy, get light from this *purāṇa*. Diving deep into the commentaries of the previous *ācāryas*, you retrieve their many treasures and, polishing those valuables with the silken cloth of your own attachment to the lotus feet of the Lord, then place them on the altarlike pages of your books for us to worship and serve.

Now you’re softly chanting *japa,* and the sound is like nectar pouring into our ears. We rejoice in thinking that our spiritual master is the very best servant of the Lord. By your chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, the world is purified. This morning a beautiful melody emanates from the harmonium in your room, and your voice is enraptured with feelings of great separation. The *Śrī Gurv-aṣṭakam* of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura is the jewel of the *maṅgala-ārati* ceremony, and you are offering this jewel at the lotus feet of your spiritual master.

Soon the sun will rise and signal the time for your morning walk. We follow behind as just one of your many devotees, but we feel like a puppy, and it is hard to keep pace, both physically and philosophically. But from our distant post you look just like the full moon surrounded by the many stars of your wonderful disciples, and you shine as their great leader. It is here where you answer the more informal and intimate inquires about the nature of things: transcendental things about Kṛṣṇa, His associates, and their loving devotional service; about *māyā* and truth; about the preaching and that very great thing which is so dear to your heart—book distribution. On these mystic walks your beaming smiles endear us to the service of the Lord, and we feel ecstatic and blessed to be with you.

You have returned to the temple, and the mighty sounds of conches reverberate. The curtains fly open, and the Lord now appears before your eyes. They are moistened with love and fixed on the transcendental forms of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vallabha. We should also look upon the Lord, but most of us are just watching you. Your lotus hands are folded in *praṇāmās,* and your head blissfully moves in appreciation and agreement with the beautiful *Brahma-saṁhitā* prayers. Slowly bowing down and then lying flat like a golden rod, you are the humble servant of the Lord. There is *caraṇāmṛta* and a towel to dry your hand, and while making your way to the *vyāsāsana* you reciprocate with all the devotees gathered here for your *darśana*. With glances of compassion and thanks you expertly acknowledge the wonderful services your followers render, and they in turn worship you as their guru by offering flowers and garlands and the *Śrī Guru-vandanā* prayers of Śrīla Narrotama Dāsa Ṭhākura. The *kīrtana* sways to the sweet beat of the *mṛdaṅgas,* and your *karatālas* chime in time. You close your eyes in thoughts of your own gurudeva, but not one of us will do the same, for you are right here before our eyes and it’s time for their perfection.

In due course the *kīrtana* gradually comes to an end and our foreheads touch the floor. From there we humbly hear from you all the glorious names of our great predecessors. Seated on the most elevated seat of the *vyāsāsana,* you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the greatest Vaiṣṇava scholar, are teaching *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which is the mature fruit of the tree of Vedic wisdom. In the form of a class, this fruit is now expertly distributed by you to the assembled devotees, who eagerly sit before you like thirsty fledglings. They taste this nectar and relish its sweetness, which has now been made even sweeter by the touch of not one Śuka but the other as well. There are some questions—some perfect, some not so perfect—but there are always perfect answers, and when the time comes to end, you thank us very much.

“Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!!”

And again the *kīrtana* resounds. Devotees dance with happy faces, and you graciously descend from the *vyāsāsana* like an *avatāra* entering the world. But the time is approaching for breakfast *prasādam,* and so you leave for your room.

Simply sitting on a mat in peaceful contemplation, you slowly and decisively taste the delicacies that have been placed before you by your loving disciples. There are three kinds of *subjīs* with hot crispy *purīs* that are golden in color and rotund in shape. There is *chidwā*, yogurt, and dried fruits. Hot *pakorās* with chutney too hot to eat but too sweet to resist. There are wonderful fruits of various kinds, like mangos, mangosteens, lychees, and longans. There are fresh figs and dark plump cherries. There are also oranges cut up in quarters, with the ends taken off just as you like them. Papayas with a dash of lime juice and cut banana decorate the plate, and if you so desire, there are sweets as well, like *burfī, rasagullā, rāsamalai,* and condensed milk. Finally, there is fresh, cool, pure, sweet water. You only take a little of what has been offered, but it is enough to give you the great strength needed for the coming day’s preaching. In your beautiful blue room, dressed in golden saffron robes and bathed in the clear morning light that streams through the colorful lead-light widows, you are picture perfect, relaxed and serene.

These are but some of the wonderful morning scenes that blessed the earth every day you were among us, and we always meditate on them as part of our daily worship. They are the heartfelt pleasure of those who were able to see them. You never stopped. You never wavered. This is just one morning out of many that changed the world as we knew it, and made us always hanker to become your eternal servants.

Your servants at ISKCON’s New Govardhana Farm Community, Murwillumbah, Australia.  
(written by Veṇugopāla Dāsa)

## New Govardhana (San Diego)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Dream—My Dream**

**Yamunā Dāsī:**Swamiji, Jānakī-devī wrote me this very nice letter where she had a dream that there was a gigantic platform above the surface of the earth, and all of our devotees, our godbrothers and -sisters, and you, were assembled on this gigantic platform for *saṅkīrtana*. And we had such a thunderous joy, magnificent *kīrtana,* that the whole earth— When you said “*Jaya oṁ paramahaṁsa*,” the whole earth bowed down to you like this. And we were all crying, so happy. And you said, “Now my Guru Mahārāja is satisfied.” That was her dream.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:**Thank you very much. Yes. Thank you for your dreaming like that. It is very pleasing to me. Yes, I want to see like that.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am a soul surrendered to you. I have no other interests. I have no desires to entertain my perishable body.

In 1990 a friend told me that if I join ISKCON I would spend the rest of my days hoeing corn and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa somewhere in the jungles of Bengal. I was fascinated. I went for it. But after the *bhakta* leader shaved my head I was told that outreach is what pleases you the most. Good bye, seclusion.

Soon I found myself selling your books on the beach in Goa, in the parks of Hong Kong, and on the street corners of Philadelphia, New York, San Francisco, and Honolulu. When I rented houses for our centers, I learned that the noisier the location the better the preaching. Instead of making my mind still (my idea of spirituality), it became flooded with numerical facts: “Thursday, Bhakta John, 18 small-soft and 10 big-hard, for a total of 72 laxmi points . . .”

I changed externally: I wrapped an orange sheet around my body instead of wearing clothes. I thought that a quarter inch of hair is *māyā*. Now I am thirty-nine and all my possessions fit in two suitcases, one with books and one with clothes. I sleep on the floor, and I have been celibate for the past seventeen years!

Such a change, such a sacrifice! Why am I doing this? Because you told me it was for my own good. I believed you 1991, and I still believe you.

Internally I changed too: I think of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya, and the Six Gosvāmīs. I am deeply affected by *harināma*. Long gone are my old ambitions; now I only want to preach to satisfy you and Lord Caitanya.

We should be fixed up on the order of Kṛṣṇa coming through the spiritual master. Then our life is successful. This is the secret of success in spiritual life. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, August 1964, Vṛndāvana\]

A devotee asked me how I preach. I answered, “I print flyers, go out to distribute them, then come back, cook *prasādam,* and host the guests.” The devotee was surprised.

Our procedure is very simple and consistent. We chant, we read from *Bhagavad-gītā* and Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, and we distribute prasādam. \[Letter to Nandarāṇī Dāsī and others, October 1967\]

Our simple process of chanting and distributing *prasādam* and selling BTG will surely make our movement successfully advance. \[Letter to Maṇḍali-bhadra Dāsa, February 1970\]

This process is the nucleus around which all our preaching strategies are grouped. Whether we preach to the scientists or the farmers, we need to go out to meet them, talk to them, and feed them *prasadam*. People will not come by themselves. Corporate America knows this well. For last year’s advertisements, American Internet companies spent $19.4 billion. Of course, we don’t have that kind of money, but we can go out and meet people. Jehovah’s Witnesses, for example, keep statistics about their evangelism. Their yearly total for the US shows 185 million preaching hours and 28,000 new members. Imagine the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement increasing by 28,000 members every year.

We must try to catch the attention of as many people as possible so that some may come and hear us and be benefited. \[Letter to Andrea Temple, March 1968\]

I wish to see that this magazine \[BTG\] is published and distributed in the American way like “Readers Digest,”“Life,” etc., published in millions and distributed all over the world. \[Letter to Guru Dāsa, April 1970\]

This is a complex age. The need (addiction) for computers and technical gadgets is deeply ingrained in the modern human’s psyche. People are overworked, stressed with family, school, social pressures, traffic jams, phobias, sleep problems, emotional conflicts, drug use, depressions, and anxieties. Life is everything but simple. Even we devotees get influenced, thinking we need a complicated approach to present Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Times are different, but the system is the same. Passing out flyers outside a health-food store is as effective now as it was thirty years ago.

We started the Kṛṣṇa Lounge here in San Diego. Gopīnātha Prabhu and Kṛṣṇa Dāsa Prabhu painted the walls earth-yellow. I got plants; Mahat-tattva Prabhu bought carpets. We made a flyer and went out to distribute it. Young people came; we sang, spoke philosophy, and distributed *prasadam*. Some young boys shaved up, others became congregational members. For the Śāstra Dāna program, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we put racks with your books in fifty places all over the city. The racks need to be refilled every few weeks. These are simple things, but they work. They work because you gave the formula.

Our process is simple, all-embracing, we have something to offer for everyone. There is chanting, dancing, feasting, and very perfect philosophy, so everyone may be satisfied with our Kṛṣṇa Consciousness Movement. Now you have traveled all over the world preaching on Lord Caitanya’s behalf, and I think you will agree that wherever you have gone, whether in South America or in the Far East, everywhere, our movement is appreciated. So I simply request you, you are doing the greatest service to the world by giving up everything for preaching this Kṛṣṇa philosophy, you please continue just as you are doing. Introduce this process of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, distribute as much *prasādam* as possible, and if possible see that all our books are translated and distributed as widely as possible. This is the greatest welfare activity, and for engaging yourself and others in this way, certainly you will go back to home, back to godhead. \[Letter to Hanumān Dāsa, October 1973\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to hold *kīrtanas* in public places, distribute your books in schools and colleges, develop the congregation, and make 28,000 new members every year. Your dream has become my dream. All your dreams were fulfilled beyond expectation, and I know with your merciful glance mine will be, too. By your grace the blind can see the stars and the lame cross mountains.

Your servants at New Govardhana, ISKCON San Diego, California, USA.  
(written by Jaya Śacīnandana Dāsa)

## New Hastināpura

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Once in Juhu, Bombay, you challenged your disciples: “How do you know that Kṛṣṇa is God? Why do you believe me? How do you know that I am not cheating you?” The devotees responded by quoting from the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* but you refuted their answers.

Finally, Mahāṁsa Prabhu shyly suggested, “Because I can feel Kṛṣṇa. I can feel Kṛṣṇa’s presence in my life.” Your eyes lit up, and you rewarded Mahāṁsa with your generous smile—“Yes, that’s it. Because you can *feel* Kṛṣṇa’s presence, that’s why you believe.” In other words, you stressed that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is personal, a dynamic relationship between the devotee and the Lord that manifests in tangible life experiences.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we want to thank you for making Kṛṣṇa consciousness so personal to us. Indeed, you taught us by example that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is all about relationships—between one devotee and another, between guru and disciple, between a devotee and the holy name, between a devotee and other living entities, and ultimately between the soul and Kṛṣṇa. How magically interrelated all these spiritual relationships are! We cannot neglect any one of them if we want to make steady spiritual progress. But because you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are our link to Kṛṣṇa, strengthening our relationship with you by following you will give us the proper foundation to deepen our personal relationships with everyone else and to recognize Lord Kṛṣṇa’s presence in our lives.

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as we continue our efforts to serve you, please help us become more personal with you. Allow us to feel your presence—while we worship you, while we read your books, while we chant the holy name, while we interact with one another, while we spread your message and serve you in many other ways. Please create the changes and the circumstances in our individual lives and in your Society in general so that we can perceive and appreciate your true divine personality, and so that we can receive, understand, and execute your desire for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Deep in our hearts we long to serve in a strong spiritual relationship with you. We know you can make this possible, by your grace.

As we gather today to celebrate your appearance, we bow down at your lotus feet with hearts full of appreciation and hope. Thank you for revealing to us who Lord Kṛṣṇa is and how to “feel” Him. Our relationship with Him starts with you. The more we serve you, the more we can feel Him. And if someone asks us why we believed you and why we chose Kṛṣṇa, the answer is simple: “Because it’s personal.”

Your servants in the New Hastināpura community in Potomac, Maryland, USA.  
(written by Divyāmbara Dāsī)

## New Kulīna-grāma

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.**

Our dearmost grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our *sāṣṭāṅga daṇḍavat praṇāmas* at the dust of Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet on this most auspicious occasion of your divine appearance. All glories to Your Divine Grace, and all glories to your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement! O most loving grandfather, please accept this heartfelt simple offering of ours as an expression of our deep love and appreciation for you.

As stated in *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.38:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have that implicit faith.

The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 22.65) describes the *uttama-adhikārīs*—great devotees—who have faith in Kṛṣṇa and their spiritual master:

*śāstra-yuktye sunipuṇa, dṛḍha-śraddhā yāṅra*  
*’uttama-adhikārī sei tāraye saṁsāra*

**“One who is expert in logic, argument, and the revealed scriptures and who has firm faith in Kṛṣṇa is classified as a topmost devotee. He can deliver the whole world.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have demonstrated that extraordinary potency to the entire world.

The Vedic scriptures state that to bring the fallen condition souls back to the spiritual world, the Lord sometimes appears Himself and at other times sends His associates. In this Age of Kali, people in general are most fallen. To save them, 522 years ago the Lord appeared in His most munificent forms, Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda, and delivered the most sinful brothers Jagāi and Mādhāi. At that time there were only two Jagāis and Mādhāis, but now the whole world is full of Jagāis and Mādhāis. So Lord Caitanya selected a very rare and special associate of His to fulfill His mission. And you came.

You dedicated everything in your possession to fulfilling the desires of the Lord and your spiritual master. You saved millions of Jagāis and Mādhāis, the most fallen and sinful souls. A great soul like Your Divine Grace always carries Kṛṣṇa in his heart and is infused with transcendental potency. By your grace and Kṛṣṇa’s, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is expanding throughout the world. We are extremely happy and grateful to know that such an extraordinary transcendental torchbearer is dispelling the darkness of ignorance among the mass of people. By our good fortune, as time passes we can see more and more clearly how exalted your position is. Your glories have been accepted by all, not only in this world but in all three worlds (*ebe yaśa ghuṣuk tri-bhuvana*).

With compassion, sweetness, and beauty, Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara and Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā Devī daily broadcast Their mercy and love to your children and grandchildren here in New Kulīna-grāma Dhāma—ISKCON Baltimore. By your grace and mercy, and with the help of your dedicated granddisciples, this community of your servants has grown and prospered in devotion to the mission of Lord Śrī Gaurasundara, who is personally residing here with His brother Nityānanda. We are trying our best to firmly emphasize our personal spiritual practice, purify our own existence, and then reach out to others. We are striving to be really sincere and humble in our efforts, to be eager to serve and encourage one another and co-operate.

Since we are inexperienced and certainly not free from the contamination of material existence, we need your mercy and guidance. Please bless us so that we may enthusiastically and happily hear and chant the holy name in the association of devotees and become free from lethargy and from desire for *lābha, pūjā,* and *pratiṣṭhā*. Please overlook our bad qualities and allow us to serve you. May our dedication to follow your instructions be engraved in our hearts till the end of our lives so that we can teach others what you have kindly given us. By our tiny preaching efforts, our lives will have some meaning, and in that way we can try to repay our debt to you. We beg you to please shower your mercy upon us and in this way bring about purity in our hearts—purity that is the force which makes everything worthwhile possible. May we always remain connected to you through our daily actions of *bhakti*. Let *jīva-dayā, nāma-ruci,* and *vaiṣṇava-sevā* be our only goals.

Kindly accept our meager offerings of our lives and service, even though full of faults and deficiencies, and always keep us under your shelter.

Your servants at New Kulīna-grāma Dhāma, ISKCON of Baltimore, Maryland, USA.  
(written by Jaya Gaurasundara Dāsa)

## New Navadvīpa

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our grateful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, whose *prasādam* (mercy) is all that we are made of!

For many years we have been selling the *prasādam* of our Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva Deities to the fortunate souls who come to our temple restaurant. In the spring of 2005 we expanded the distribution to the University of Hawaii, where students and professors alike have become addicted to the taste of your mercy. Every weekday, from 10 a.m. to 2 p.m., they line up at our kiosk at the campus Sustainability Courtyard to purchase a good selection of vegan and vegetarian entrees, soups, salads, savories, and drinks.

“The food is healthy, tasty, and reasonably priced,” says a nutrition student who has been eating with us the last two years. “There’s no food like this anywhere. I hope you never leave.”

An English professor taking the *prasādam* back to her office says, “I like the mint-ginger-lemon tea and the opportunity to get healthy snacks between classes. I get nourished without being weighed down.” Room for improvement? “Stay open longer so I can snack later after class.”

A first-timer just finishing her plate found us by chance, she thinks. She usually walks another way to class, but today “Govinda’s Pure Vegetarian Cuisine” caught her eye. She vows to keep coming.

A group of students tick off their favorites—garden burgers, eggplant parmesan, lasagna, and the Thai yellow curry. They miss the sweet breads we use to make but aren’t complaining: *prasādam* is *prasādam.*

A lady who has been coming since we opened appreciates the vegan options and especially likes the *aloha* with which the food is prepared and served. *Aloha* is the Hawaiian word for *bhakti—*love and devotion. This customer has tasted the essence of *prasādam.*

In February 2007 we opened a second location downtown, catering to the students at Hawaii Pacific University and the workers in the big office buildings nearby. “Govindajīs” has become the darling of the downtown vegetarian lunch crowd.

A student says he always eats here before an exam, to feel nourished and alert. An elderly diabetic man appreciates the healthy fare at a good price. A lady says she’s not a vegetarian but thanks to our food she’s on her way. She notes the surprising lack of veggie restaurants in Honolulu and wishes, like the English professor, that we could be open longer.

Maybe someday we could, if the Lord sends more devotees and facilities. Meanwhile, our small crew of devotees is doing an outstanding job, week after week, of preparing *prasādam* for the Deities and five hundred hungry souls. And whatever *prasādam* is left we take straight to a homeless shelter as Hare Krishna Food for Life. Recently we received a letter of appreciation from the shelter’s Chief Executive Officer:

The organic and vegetarian food items have been an enormous help in supplementing our meals served to homeless clientele. We are truly grateful for your support. . . . Offering nutritious meals to this vulnerable, hard-to-reach group is a perfect entree to engaging them in Safe Haven services. How fortunate we are to be a beneficiary of your services.

And how fortunate we are to be engaged in your everlasting loving service, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your *prasādam* (mercy) is all that we are made of. And our *aloha,* our *bhakti,* increases as we share your mercy with others. All glories to Your Divine Grace! Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your grateful servants at New Navadvīpa, Honolulu, Hawaii, USA.

## New Ramaṇa-reti

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, and all glories to this special day, which brings us together to meditate on, appreciate, and share with each other all that you have given us.

The New Ramaṇa-reti community itself exemplifies one aspect of your gifts, the building of “a house where the whole world can live.” Here many generations and nationalities of your spiritual children, grandchildren, and great-grandchildren come together to take shelter from the harsh hand of Kali-yuga. We encourage each other with recollections of your motto “simple living and high thinking,” with beautiful worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Your Divine Grace, and with daily classes and enthusiastic discussions on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Here we have grand annual festivals, weekly *harināma-saṅkīrtana*, home *bhajana* programs, and frequent opportunities to hear from your senior men and women. Our own New Ramaṇa-reti Kṛṣṇa Day School is growing more and more, there are *nāma-haṭṭa* outreach programs with the extended Indian community, there are traveling Ratha-yātrā festivals throughout our state . . . and thus the good life you have given us goes on and on.

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have taught us how to live and how to die, how to give and how to take Kṛṣṇa conscious association, how to care for each other and how to be a friend to everyone. Add to this the *sādhana* program you instructed us to follow and the regulative principles of freedom you taught us, and we see why this community life is so satisfying.

And just as you are always present in our lives, through a prayer, a thought, or a Bhakitvedanta purport, so we are forever in your debt. Today, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are unified in our desire to make payments on our eternal debt to you. May we keep focused on preaching and reaching out to others, as you unceasingly did. May we work in a cooperative spirit to develop and refine our ISKCON center in Alachua. And may we always think, “What would Śrīla Prabhupāda do?”

Your servants at New Ramaṇa-reti dhāma, ISKCON Alachua, Florida, USA.

## New Remuṇā

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā!

On this holy day of your appearance, your servants at New Remuṇā*dhāma* want to take this opportunity to say thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us the essence of the Vedic teachings through your books, letters, oral instructions, and most of all by your own glorious example. Thank you for leaving behind the most desirable spiritual world and appearing on this planet to save lost souls like me. Thank you for illuminating the path back home, back to Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what you have done for this world is simply not possible describe. You are Kṛṣṇa’s empowered ambassador who brought the light of transcendental Vedic knowledge into our lives while we struggled in the darkness of ignorance. You are *mahā-patita-pāvana;* you saved countless souls from hellish conditions simply with your boundless love and compassion.

I was a wandering soul. Although I had everything in the material sense, I felt something was missing in my life. I was simply not happy. One day a young enthusiastic devotee walked into my store and handed me a *Kṛṣṇa* book. With that, my spiritual journey began, a journey of ecstatic love and joy. A single *Kṛṣṇa* book saved me from material drudgery and changed my life forever. Similarly, numerous fallen souls have been saved by the gift of your transcendental books.

In 1971, the enthusiastic devotees tirelessly served Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission in the small house at 187 Gerrard Street. Responding to your prayers, Kṛṣṇa then gave us our present temple building. We faced many obstacles after your disappearance, but we persevered because you had planted the seed of *bhakti* in us and given us the taste of devotional service to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Kṣīra-cora-Gopīnātha. With your mercy we are now worshiping Their Lordships in this beautiful, grand temple in the heart of downtown Toronto, attracting more and more sincere followers. Thank you for this wonderful gift.

We are still working hard to maintain the temple nicely. By your grace, the temple—your home—is now mortgage-free, and we promise we will never mortgage your house again. This house is blessed by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please empower us to use this temple and all of our other resources to spread this sacred movement to every town and village. Your granddisciples are actively participating in temple activities here, and all our festivals are a big success, as evinced by our burgeoning congregation.

On a personal note, I feel blessed and extremely fortunate to have seen you with my eyes, touched your lotus feet, and heard you utter my name with your lotus mouth. These things in themselves have made my life complete. I humbly beg for your blessings to be able to continue wholeheartedly engaging myself in sincerely serving your forever-expanding ISKCON family. Please bless me with intelligence and spiritual strength to serve you the way you desire, in this life and beyond.

Your servants at New Remuṇā Dhama, Toronto, Canada.  
(written by Subuddhi Devī Dāsī)

## New Seva Kuñja

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are most unqualified to write an homage to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your transcendental appearance in this world. However, very mercifully, your dear disciple His Holiness Candramauli Swami has requested us to write this offering to you, representing all the devotees connected with the ISKCON Chicago *yātrā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the depth and extent of the gifts you have given this world cannot be estimated, even by highly exalted souls. Your books, your temples, the *saṅkīrtana* process, your transcendental instructions on virtually all practical, philosophical, and devotional topics, your ISKCON, the opportunity for worshiping the Deities of Kṛṣṇa, endless opportunities for serving in a variety of ways, the empowerment of your followers to represent your pristinely pure teachings, the courage to live a life of pure devotion, the daily opportunity to accept *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*—this is only a sampling of your gifts. You gave us these gifts at great personal risk and while undergoing difficulties of all sorts.

The greatest gifts you have left behind for future generations are your most committed representatives, who continue to guide us lovingly in so many ways in assimilating your pure teachings and living by them. At New Seva Kuñja (ISKCON Chicago) we are blessed with the association of many visiting senior devotees. In particular, we are blessed by our GBC representatives His Holiness Romapāda Swami and His Holiness Candramauli Swami. (Candramauli Swami has very kindly made our temple his headquarters.) By their example and precepts, they are teaching us the ways of loving exchanges among the temple devotees and the congregation members. Their loving guidance is giving us great strength and inspiration for understanding and applying your teachings, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In your purport to the fourth verse of *The Nectar of Instruction*, you write: “Simply by reciprocating in the six ways mentioned above, an ordinary man can fully revive his Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” In this simple yet profound statement you have given a blueprint for ISKCON’s goals, methodology, and activities. You have taught us that the efficacy of a process can be understood by how the followers of the process are developing love of Kṛṣṇa. Love of Kṛṣṇa includes love for His devotees and all the other parts and parcels of Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, at ISKCON Chicago we are poised to make a determined effort toward a true culture of love and trust based on this verse of the *Upadeśāmṛta*. If people at large participate in such a loving culture, they will naturally give up their tendencies for material enjoyment and mutual animosity toward one another. Even an onlooker will become attracted to Kṛṣṇa consciousness by this culture. ISKCON’s and your true glories will spread and touch the core of the people’s hearts, resulting in a revolution in their otherwise impious lives.

Specifically, in an effort to nurture such a loving atmosphere, we have identified three focus areas: (1) offering genuine spiritual care to all devotees connected to the temple, (2) offering the best services possible to the presiding Deities at the temple, and (3) imparting education in the six loving exchanges at our Sunday Feast programs.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are completely dependent on your mercy to make pleasing progress in these areas. We are like young children with many limitations and defects. Kindly bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so we can please your direct servants through these efforts and thus please you.

Your insignificant servants at New Seva Kuñja, Chicago, Illinois, USA.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa Smaraṇa Dāsa)

## New Tālavan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you and your spiritual master!

Today is the appearance day of Lord Nityānanda, the original spiritual master. I pray for the mercy of Nityānanda Prabhu to properly glorify you, my eternal spiritual master and the eternal master of your International Society for Krishna Consciousness. May we satisfy you every day of our lives.

We are so lucky to have come into your association. Otherwise, we would have remained lost in the material nature. Unlike ourselves, our children were born into your mercy. Hopefully they’ll never lose that great fortune. Hopefully we will not fall again into the darkness of forgetfulness of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us always have the opportunity to serve your lotus feet by fulfilling your orders. Others may identify some other person as the object of their love, but you are our loving lord and master eternally. You are also our best friend. However, you are not an ordinary friend. You are the representative of the Supreme Lord, and consequently our relationship with you is always one of great respect and veneration. Please give us your orders.

As we enter another year, we want to rededicate ourselves to your mission. Here we worship the Supreme Lord and you as you have taught us. We protect mother cow and father bull. We educate our children in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We try to grow our own food and distribute your books. We do many of the same things your disciples do around the world. We want to do them better and better so that you and Lord Govinda will become very pleased by our service. This is our mode—to please you by our humble service.

This humbleness is a character trait, and that is another way we are attempting to please you. We are trying to model ourselves into the persons you want us to be: humble and tolerant, respectful and free from pride and false prestige. That one become such an excellent being is recommended by both you and Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the twelfth chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and in so many other places in your bona fide translations of the topmost books of Vedic knowledge. Yet I am still contaminated by the demoniac qualities described by Lord Kṛṣṇa in the sixteenth chapter of His *Gītā*. We pray for your special mercy to relinquish our bad behavior and adopt better behavior. You have given us the proper values and means to make this possible.

Thank you for *bhagavat-prasādam*, the holy names of the Supreme Being, the proper understanding of the relations between the sexes, and the directions and information of the scriptures. Thus we have renounced our speculative habits and diet, taken up living properly with a spouse, and rejected the many stimulants available. Your directions are responsible for giving us these values and lifestyle so that we can make our characters everlastingly good. Ultimately, you gave us our relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Although it had been lost, forgotten, abandoned, and even abused, by following your directions we are reviving that relationship. But our success all depends on pleasing you, for without pleasing you there is no way for us to please Bhagavān and make spiritual advancement (*Gurvaṣṭakam* 8). Thus if we displease you we will have to remain in the mixed modes of material nature, exhibiting inferior behavior and remaining forgetful of the Absolute Truth. But just by following your directions, we have changed direction and are becoming godly.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, we want to satisfy you, as so many of your disciples and granddisciples have satisfied you by their tremendous devotional service. May Lord Nityānanda, on His appearance day, bless us to also fulfill your desires. May we make New Tālavana a transcendental place of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, where cows are loved and protected, families appreciated, and renunciants respected. May we develop the facilities for people to come here and comfortably appreciate your temple, restaurant, landscaping, pastures, woodlands, and guest accommodations. May your disciples and granddisciples be pleased by our development of this ISKCON project and appreciate and praise its existence. May we all love one another with transcendental vision and perceive you and Kṛṣṇa in the center of it all.

We conclude this humble offering, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by thanking you 16,108 times for the opportunity to work for your pleasure and practice your teachings. There is nothing better than this.

Your servants at New Tālavana dhāma, Mississippi, USA.  
(written by Yogīndra-vandana Dāsa)

## New Varṣāṇā

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories unto you! Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Anyone who seriously takes up even one of the processes of devotional service given by you in your ISKCON society can obtain the real goal of the human form of life and return home, back to Godhead.

For example, the worship of Śrīmatī Tulasī Devī simultaneously destroys our sins and bestows all spiritual benedictions upon us. As we read in the beautiful prayers of Candraśekhara Kavi, when we see Tulasī Devī or touch her, all our sins are destroyed. Whoever takes shelter of her has their wishes fulfilled, and she makes them a resident of Vṛndāvana. By the mercy of Tulasī Devī we can behold the beautiful pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. We can beg Śrīmati Tulasī Devī to make us a follower of the cowherd damsels of Vraja and pray that she makes us her own maidservant. Lord Śiva, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, Nārada Muni, and all the *jñānīs* and great *munis,* headed by Lord Brahmā, circumambulate her. Following in your footsteps, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we also have the divine opportunity to do the same.

In addition, you have given us your transcendental literatures, such as *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*; Deity worship; the holy *dhāmas* of Māyāpur, Vṛndāvana, and Jagannātha Purī; the Ganges, the Yamunā, Rādhā-kuṇḍa, Śyāma-kuṇḍa, Śrī Govardhana Hill, and the dust of the *dhāma,* as well as the association of saintly devotees and the holy name of the Lord—all of which can bestow their mercy.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have set our goals and given us the means to achieve them. What else do we need? All we have to do is follow your instructions and, by your mercy, let the process of devotional service work.

As the iron rod becomes fire by its association with fire, so we become pure by association with you and with everything else within your ISKCON.

Your servants at New Varṣāṇā, Auckland, Australia.  
(written by Kalasaṁvara Dāsa)

## New Vraja-dhāma (Hungary)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances All glories to you on this wonderful day of your appearance!

The building of New Vraja-dhāma began fifteen years ago here in Hungary, under the leadership of His Holiness Śivarāma Swami. This is a short period in the life of a society. Still, we have realized many things and gained a lot of experience while striving to realize one of your main wishes: the *varṇāśrama* system in Kali-yuga.

On the occasion of the hundredth anniversary of your appearance, in 1996, Śivarāma Mahārāja invited Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, Śrī Govardhana-lāla, and Gaura-naṭarāja Mahāprabhu to stay with us. They mercifully obliged and came to us in their wonderful Deity forms. They occupied their newly built temple, and Their worship began. The devotees busied themselves making every square inch of the farm increasingly beautiful: the temple, the cow shed, the ploughed lands, the organic garden, the apiary, the *gurukula,* the guesthouse, and everything else. As we tried to depend more and more on the land and the cows—under the careful guidance of Śivarāma Mahārāja—more and more details of the *daivī-varṇāśrama* gradually began to appear before our eyes.

The famous prayer from the *Viṣṇu Purāṇa* (1.19.65) became even more meaningful for us over the years:

*namo brahmaṇya-devāya go-brāhmaṇa-hitāya ca*  
*jagad-dhitāya kṛṣṇāya govindāya namo namaḥ*

**“Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the worshipable Deity for all brahminical men, who is the well-wisher of cows and brāhmaṇas, and who is always benefiting the whole world. I offer my repeated obeisances to the Personality of Godhead, known as Kṛṣṇa and Govinda.”**

When a Kṛṣṇa conscious society has the worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara at its core, and when taking care of the cows, the devotees and everything connected to them is organically related to this worship, then that society will flourish both spiritually and materially.

When we take good care of the cows, they will serve the Divine Couple and the devotees with their milk. If we engage the oxen in agricultural work, the result will be beautiful and healthy fruits and vegetables that we can offer on the altar every day. Devotees will be happy and healthy when they can honor *prasādam* prepared from such foodstuffs.

Protecting children, women, and the elderly is also a very important factor in a Kṛṣṇa conscious society. The protection of women secures the birth of children who are in a good state of mind and who have the right family background. The children who can grow up among such circumstances will form a more chaste generation than ours, and they will be the ones to carry on the movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Elderly devotees who leave their bodies here will certainly go back to the spiritual world—or, if they are really fortunate, they will be born here in New Vraja-dhāma to continue serving Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara and your perfect mission.

Looking at all of this from an economic point of view, one could say that instead of protecting the cows it would be cheaper just to buy milk in the shop, instead of tilling the land it would be cheaper to buy food at the market, and we would lower our expenses if we did not maintain a whole school for the sake of just a few children. These investments do not seem profitable, but our experience is to the contrary.

Only by trying to follow everything we have learned from you and from your dear disciples do we find that Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara always kindly help us so we can nicely maintain this beautiful realm of Theirs.

Śrīla Prabhupāda! We would like to thank you for mercifully coming to this material world and making every place a Vṛndāvana where your enthusiastic followers strive to realize all the different parts of your Kṛṣṇa conscious mission.

Your servants in New Vraja-dhāma, Hungary.

## New Vrajamaṇḍala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your dedicated followers! All glories to ISKCON!

As we look around, we see your mercy flowing constantly around us. We just need to extend our hand and catch it. You created a big revolution in our lives and gave us the highest process of self-realization: the chanting of the holy names of Kṛṣṇa. You created a proper atmosphere for us to chant: the association of pure devotees. You gave us the key to avoid troubles: the four regulative principles. You gave us a big source of inspiration: your transcendental books. You gave us everything. But it is still up to us to take advantage of so much mercy.

We have a strong tendency to get carried away by society, work, etc., and neglect your instructions on how to be happy. We get so worried about so many things and forget to worry about your desires.

This year in New Vrajamaṇḍala we are celebrating the twenty-fifth anniversary of the installation of our loving Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda-candra. This is a very special event for us, and many of the citizens of this small community are preparing offerings for their Lordships. Some of them are as follows.

The Sunday Feast program is back to help us focus on the preaching. Many guests come around and are nicely cared for by enthusiastic devotees.

We are putting energy into going out on *harināma* and organizing festivals for the pleasure of their Lordships and the devotees.

Cow protection is exemplary at New Vrajamaṇḍala. Happy, loving cows surrounded by loving devotees and happy children are gladly giving their lovely milk for the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa.

The fields are cared for by responsible devotees who have come forward with enthusiasm and dedication.

We also want to offer a new temple for Their Lordships for Their Silver Jubilee. A great source of good fortune for many souls is to engage in intimate service to the Lord in His Deity form.

We are making a great effort to chant *japa* together. This is proving to be a miraculous balm and a source of unlimited happiness and enthusiasm. We don’t need to invent new things; you already gave us everything.

This year we are especially meditating on these words of yours: “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate with each other after I am gone.” We are the servants of the servants of your servants, and we are ready to give you lots of happiness by working together and pushing on this movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by making your mercy available to as many people as possible. This is our humble offering. We are trying to sacrifice our egos and self-interest for your pleasure.

We all came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness by your mercy; we are a product of your mercy, and to your mercy we belong. We don’t want anything else. Please allow us to appreciate that mercy; send us your inspiring followers to keep us alive in your mercy.

Without your mercy we have no hope; we will never be able to fulfill any of our goals.

We aspire to be the servants of the servants of your servants.  
The devotees at New Vrajamaṇḍala, Spain.

## New Vrindaban

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

At 2:00 a.m. one September day in 1962, a ray of light burst forth from a small room in the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndāvana, India. On this occasion of your appearance we are reminded of this light, for this was the light you lit to translate the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Because of your selfless sacrifice at 2:00 in the morning, your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* purports now shine forth with the light that can guide humanity back home, back Godhead. As you sat down at your desk in that sacred room, on a straw mat covering a hard sandstone floor, you contemplated how to benefit everyone in this age of quarrel and hypocrisy. Similarly, 450 years earlier Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu had sat in His home in Navadvīpa and contemplated: “I have come to deliver all the fallen souls, but now just the opposite has happened. How can these rogues be delivered? How can they be benefited?” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 17.262).

At the present moment you are sitting at your desk in the study room of your Palace of Gold in New Vrindāban and contemplating these same thoughts. As you bend forward and continue writing, your pen is inscribing on the hearts of you disciples and granddisciples the inspiration to push forward the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

On this auspicious day of your appearance, as we offer flowers at your lotus feet, we beg Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vṛndāvana-candra to bless us so that we may always take guidance from the light shining forth from every word you have written in the pages of your books. We pray They will help us learn to live those words and share those words with others. We pray They will bestow upon us wisdom, patience, and enthusiasm so we can properly take of care of Their cows, Their devotees, Their guests, and Their places of pastimes here in New Vrindāban.

Today we pray for the spiritual strength and humility that are found in the dust of your lotus feet. By placing this transcendental dust on our heads, we will become empowered to always take shelter of the association of devotees and love one another with the same compassion, respect, and joy with which you love us all.

We beg to remain your humble servants.

Your disciples and granddisciples in New Vrindāban, West Virginia, USA.

## New York (Brooklyn)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to Your Divine Grace, the most wonderful devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa we ever had the good fortune to meet and receive love, mercy, and guidance from!

In the fifth verse of the great spiritual realization of Śrī Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmī (*Manaḥ-śikṣā*), it is stated: “My dear mind, in this material life you are attacked by lust, anger, greed, and so on, which are like a gang of thugs who waylay innocent pilgrims, and this gang is strangling you with strong, terrible ropes in the form of distress, pain, and futile endeavors. In this condition you must cry out piteously and invoke the mercy of the unalloyed devotees of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the killer of the demon Baka. They will surely protect you from this dilemma.”

In his *Śrī Bhajana-darpaṇa,* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura writes, “Lust, anger, greed, illusion, arrogance, and envy are the six thugs who openly harass the living entity. As if in conspiracy, they attack the innocent *jīva* traveling through the different stages of life.”

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (2.62–63) Śrī Kṛṣṇa says, “While contemplating the objects of the senses, a person develops attachment for them, and from such attachment lust develops, and from lust anger arises. From anger, complete delusion arises, and from delusion bewilderment of memory. When memory is bewildered, intelligence is lost, and when intelligence is lost one falls down again into the material pool.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you illuminate the topmost goals for your loyal followers, both *śikṣā* and *dīkṣā,* so they can more easily achieve them. Sincere endeavors to achieve those goals are accompanied by the special mercy of support by ŚrīNityānanda Rāma, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Śrīmati Rādhārāṇī, Śrīla Govindadeva, and well-wishing Vaiṣṇavas

In your own wise words, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you reveal the true path:

So here Kṛṣṇa says, *tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti*. If the living entity does not enter into another gross body, then where does he go? So Kṛṣṇa says, *mām eti.* “He comes to Me.” Now just imagine what is the speed of spirit soul, just after leaving this body after death, he immediately goes to Goloka Vṛndāvana, or at least where Kṛṣṇa is there. *Mām eti.* Kṛṣṇa’s *nitya-līlā,* that has been described in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* how Kṛṣṇa’s *nitya-līlā* is going on. Kṛṣṇa is present in a *bhauma-līlā* in one of the universes every time. The Lord Caitanya has given the example as the movement of the sun. The sun is moving, and the twenty-four hours into sixty minutes, into sixty seconds. . . The. . . All these time changes are there, every time. Just like our sunrise was, say, at six thirty, or six. This six thirty passed here, but somewhere else it is now six thirty. Again when it will pass six thirty there, in somewhere else there will be six thirty. Similarly Kṛṣṇa is passing from one universe to another, and when He’s visible in this universe, we find Kṛṣṇa’s appearance. So Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī has informed us that those who are competent, those who have become competent to associate with Kṛṣṇa, as Kṛṣṇa says, *tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti* . . . This *mām eti,* that spirit soul goes to Kṛṣṇa first of all within some universe where Kṛṣṇa is having His pastimes, *bhauma-līlā.* He goes there. Then just like when a person is trained up as apprentice, then he’s given the post. Just like a magistrate, after passing the examination, I.C.S. examination, he’s trained up under some magistrate, and then he’s given the post. Similarly, a living entity, when he is actually eligible by Kṛṣṇa consciousness, by development of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then immediately after giving up this body, he’s transferred to that universe where Kṛṣṇa is there. *Bhauma-līlā.* And after his being trained up thoroughly, then he’s transferred to the original Kṛṣṇaloka, Goloka Vṛndāvana. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, Vṛndāvana, 2 November 1972\]

The above explanation of the ultimate benediction and greatest possible elevation of the *jīvātmā* in favorable Kṛṣṇa consciousness, elevation of the “competent” and sincere disciples and devotees who, by the unparalleled mercy of Guru and Gaurāṅga, have this indescribable good fortune and exclusive golden opportunity to attain to the topmost divine location with the divine association of Śrī Kṛṣṇa in His *bhauma-līlā*—this explanation is indeed an illuminating, loud, and crystal-clear wake-up call. We can achieve this boon only by your showering of love and mercy upon us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your Divine Grace wisely guides us with such appropriate intensity that you kindle the flame of devotion and inspire sincerity and seriousness within the hearts of countless conditioned souls. Thus they become determined to use whatever remaining time is destined for these imperfect and destructible machines of material energy called human bodies to capably function and support the devotional initiatives of the spirit soul within.

My deep and urgent concerns, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are (1) that too many of us may not be comprehending these loving devotional alerts of truth and wisdom you most mercifully provide for us and (2) that we may not be utilizing your mercy in all seriousness by rendering pure devotional service in strict adherence to such absolutely wise guidance. Especially considering the serious and dangerous problems the powerful time factor presents, a factor that destroys all worldly material things and may sometimes appear as insurmountable opposition to our rapid spiritual progress, we must make proper use of the remaining short time we have to achieve the required devotional attitude and loving mood of selfless surrender. In this way we can qualify ourselves to be considered, as you say, “competent” in our devotional service to guru and Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the majority of us, your devotional servants with lesser or greater degrees of competent service and surrender, our time isn’t long. Please help us; we are rapidly approaching the final test of our *bhajana.* Of course, at all times we urgently need to remain one hundred percent dependent on your special mercy to attain a favorable devotional attitude and to successfully reach the goals you want to happily see us achieve. Please wake us from our deep humiliating sleep of ignorance. Chastise us with your cane of loving mercy. We desperately need your special oversight, your special mercy. The sun is setting, and midnight is rapidly approaching. The illusory riders of mundane destiny are approaching fast. Please be kind to all of us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We love you, our real father, friend, and eternal master.

We fervently implore all aspiring Kṛṣṇa devotees to sooner rather than later cast aside all impediments, all *anarthas* embedded in the heart blocking the flow of sincere devotion, so we may end this material sojourn of misery on the highest, sweetest, and most harmonious *bhajana* chord. We may sound this most loving devotional note in praise of your divine lotus feet by following sincerely your wise guidance. Adherence to the Vedic principles you gave us brings the competence required to attract and receive the special mercy of ŚrīNityānanda Rāma, the unparalleled deliverer of the unqualified souls from the turbulent and merciless ocean of birth and death, and thus receive the matchless gift of elevation to the *bhauma-līlā* of Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

We beg—with a deep realization of our spiritual incompetence, both individually and collectively, knowing we are less than a perfectly unified devotional team—to remain your insignificant servants forever. Please bless all of us with your love and mercy forever, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your special mercy makes the wonderful goal of serving Śrī Kṛṣṇa in His all-attractive supreme abode, Vṛndāvana, a real possibility, even for fallen, unqualified, and insignificant souls like us. We must grasp the necessity of achieving such a goal and realize the concomitant difficulty of achieving such an elevated status of existence. We must become motivated to cast off all weakness and attachment and to surrender fully with love and devotion to your divine instructions, your divine lotus feet, and your divine, loving, pure heart. In one sense, the following important *śloka* of absolute truth that Śrī Kṛṣṇa spoke in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (8.21) is like an exit door from this material world of misery, a door leading to the supreme abode of eternity, knowledge, and bliss:

*avyakto ’kṣara ity uktas tam āhuḥ paramāṁ gatim*  
*yaṁ prāpya na nivartante tad dhāma paramaṁ mama*

**“That which the Vedāntists describe as unmanifest and infallible, that which is known as the supreme destination, that place from which, having attained it, one never returns—that is My supreme abode.”**

The supreme abode of the Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, is described in the *Brahma-saṁhitā* as *cintāmaṇi-dhāma,* a place where all desires are fulfilled. The supreme abode of Lord Kṛṣṇa, known as Goloka Vṛndāvana, is full of palaces made of touchstone. There are also trees, called “desire trees,” that supply any type of eatable upon demand, and there are cows, known as *surabhi* cows, which supply a limitless supply of milk. In this abode, the Lord is served by hundreds of thousands of goddesses of fortune (Lakṣmīs), and He is called Govinda, the primal Lord and the cause of all causes. The Lord is accustomed to blow His flute (*veṇuṁ kvaṇantam*). His transcendental form is the most attractive in all the worlds — His eyes are like lotus petals, and the color of His body is like the color of clouds. He is so attractive that His beauty excels that of thousands of Cupids. He wears saffron cloth, a garland around His neck and a peacock feather in His hair. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* Lord Kṛṣṇa gives only a small hint of His personal abode, Goloka Vṛndāvana, which is the supermost planet in the spiritual kingdom. A vivid description is given in the *Brahma-saṁhitā.* Vedic literatures (*Kaṭha Upaniṣad* 1.3.11) state that there is nothing superior to the abode of the Supreme Godhead, and that that abode is the ultimate destination (*puruṣān na paraṁ kiñcit sā kāṣṭhā paramā gatiḥ*). When one attains to it, he never returns to the material world.

Please guide us with the power of your wisdom, mercy, and love through this transcendental door. Grab hold of our hand from the other side of this door, which leads to true freedom, and bring us to your dear Lord and Master Śrī Kṛṣṇa with our consciousness perfected in love and devotion. Please deliver us to the topmost platform of Kṛṣṇa conscious realization and wisdom and save us from the burning fire of materialism we remain trapped in and suffering from, birth after birth.

Your insignificant servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindadeva Mandira, Brooklyn, New York, USA.  
(written by Rāmabhadra Dāsa)

## New York (26 Second Avenue)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet!

We are truly very blessed to have the opportunity to serve here at Matchless Gifts, 26 Second Avenue. Often we don’t realize the significance of this historic site, which you worked so hard to establish, until we encounter a devotee from out of town who expresses his or her surprise and wonder at beholding this magical little storefront “where it all began.” Once I saw a devotee step out of a cab right in front of the temple, look at the **facade** of the building, and break down in tears. Incidents such as this keep reminding us of the unique and unparalleled significance of 26 Second Avenue.

We now have ten *brahmacārīs* living in our Manhattan *āśrama,* and each one is using his unique talent to spread the movement. Because of your desire to reach out to the public in New York, we have three *brahmacārīs* who are inspired to distribute books in Manhattan. During the December 2007 marathon, they and others worked tirelessly to distribute over 2,800 books in New York City. For all of 2007 they distributed more than 14,000 books. You had also expressed a deep desire to reach out to the young, intelligent people in the universities. You said we should preach to the higher class, and that they in turn would preach to the masses. You demonstrated your satisfaction by glorifying Bhakti-svarūpa Dāmodara Mahārāja’s achieving a Ph.D. By your causeless mercy we have nine weekly programs with college students. Just as so many young people were fed by you at 26 Second Avenue, students from various nearby colleges are coming to take *prasādam* at the storefront four days a week.

We are feeling extremely inspired to be living and preaching here in Manhattan. You meditated on coming to New York even while you lived in the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndāvana:

I planned that I must go to America. . . . I was simply thinking how to go to New York. . . . I was scheming whether “I shall go this way, through Tokyo, Japan, or that way? Which way is cheaper?” That was my plan. And I was targeting to New York always. Sometimes I was dreaming that I have come to New York. \[Morning Walk Conversation, March 1976, Māyāpur\]

Again, in a letter to Ṛṣi Kumar Dāsa in April of 1970, you stated:

New York is the best city of your country; therefore the prospect of preaching in New York City is especially very great. I began my preaching life in this city; therefore I have got some special attraction for New York.

Knowing that this city was targeted by you and became the focal point of your thoughts before you came West gives us tremendous inspiration and enthusiasm to continue the work you left behind. In fact, that is what keeps us connected to you. New York City is often referred to as “the heart of *māyā,*” and there is no doubt that none of us would be living here were it not for the service you have provided for us. We hope and pray at your lotus feet that in a mood of cooperation and humility we can dedicate our lives to preaching here in Manhattan and fulfilling your desire to establish a powerful culture of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to attract the hearts and minds of the local people.

Your servants at Matchless Gifts, 26 Second Avenue, New York, New York, USA.

## Noida

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet! All glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā day! Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, every year on this most auspicious day we express our gratitude at your lotus feet by informing you of our previous year’s tiny efforts toward pushing on your divine movement. This reminds us of a pastime of yours: One year when your dear disciple Bhakti Tīrtha Swami was submitting a report of his services at your lotus feet, you listened calmly and reciprocated with one sentence: “Kṛṣṇa is the doer.” Your each and every written or spoken word is meant to instruct all of us. We pray at your lotus feet so that as we render some service we may always remember that Kṛṣṇa is the doer.
