We should write an homage and submit a report to you not only once a year for the Vyāsa-pūjā book but every day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are still in the process of completing your project at Noida—the Śrī Śrī Rādhā Govinda-deva Mandir and Bhaktivedanta Academy for Spiritual Science. We have almost completed the overall structure of the building and are now moving toward the finishing phase: putting in the marble flooring, the ceiling, and the artwork, and painting the walls, installing the carving work, etc. Due to our incompetence, the project is taking longer than it should have. We desperately need your blessings so that we can inaugurate the project by Kārttika 2009.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, at this moment the diorama work is going on. We have some expert artists from Orissa and Bengal working on the 3D projections of artwork on *kṛṣṇa-līlā* for different floors. The front elevation stonework is progressing. Our temporary Govinda’s Restaurant has been set up in a small room outside the temple, facing the main road, and is doing exceptionally well. Many working people turn up for lunch and dinner *prasādam*. They appreciate the quality of the *prasādam* and the peaceful environment. We are trying to speed up the pace of construction to save *lakṣmī*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your Noida temple is becoming famous for organizing big festivals and educational programs. Recently we organized a seminar week to educate our congregation. With your blessings we enrolled 150 devotees. We had morning and evening sessions so that office-goers could attend before going to work and then come back again for the evening session. Devotees become fired up after completing such courses as “How to Make Progress in Bhakti,” by Praśāntā Mātājī, “Parenting,” by Ānanda Vṛndāvaneśvarī Mātājī, “The Gṛhastha Āśrama,” by His Grace Kratu Prabhu, “Śikṣāṣṭaka,” by His Holiness Lokanāth Swami, “The Nectar of Instruction,” by His Holiness Gopāl Krishna Goswami, “Śrīla Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta” by His Grace Mahamān Prabhu, and “The Guru-Disciple Relationship,” by His Holiness Candramauli Swami. Also, there were special courses designed for young boys and girls by Gopāṅgā Mātājīs team from Nagpur.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you always emphasized preaching; educating; publishing, reading, and distributing books; and engaging everyone in the loving devotional service of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. Since the Noida project has a unique feature in the Bhaktivedanta Academy, we are focusing more on systematic education of the devotees in scripture. The Bhakti Vṛkṣa program is spreading nicely in various homes and developing more and more sincere devotees for temple services. The number of youths is increasing. The Prahlāda School is doubling the children every year. The standard of the ladies’ program is improved; they are more enthusiastic for reading and serving now. We are in the middle of discussions aiming at having some VIHE courses taught at the Noida temple.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please guide us through the development of this project just as you guided your first batch of devotees at the Juhu temple. We also need your kind attention, step-by-step guidance, support, and of course mercy so that we can not only complete the building quickly but can also fill it with sincere devotees who will help the souls lost trapped in today’s glamorous but degrading modern society.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā *mahotsava-kī jaya*!  
Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Noida, India.

## Padayātrā (India)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Our All-India Padayātrā group is walking on. We are now in Tamilnadu and going north toward Karnataka.

There are so many activities that we could be doing in this world . . . so many! We could be selling tea in an Indian train, driving a rickshaw in Chennai, or be a stressed-out business manager in Mumbai. But somehow or other, by your oceanic mercy, we have been given this great service of walking in the footsteps of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, dancing, and selling books.

Your Padayātrā program is a strong one. It is so healthy for the body, mind, and soul. We are doing the same activities every day, so the mind becomes very peaceful and surcharged with spiritual energy. We are getting physically strong and healthy from all the exercise, and our souls are free—free from all the alluring sense objects of the big cities.

We are now twenty *brahmacārīs* walking, and we feel so well-protected in one another’s association and the association of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara and you. Padayātrā is the best place for us to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness and serve your lotus feet. We are experiencing your mercy and the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu every day, and all this mercy keeps us invigorated and ecstatic!

The other day, as we came into a small town near Tirunelveli, in Tamilnadu, we met a butcher sitting on the roadside with his axe, doing his nefarious activities. It was a most unpleasant sight, and we hurried past him and went on to approach some townspeople standing a little further down the road. One of these people was very interested in your *Bhagavad-gītā,* and he eagerly inquired about the price. When the price was settled, he took us back to where we had just come from and went right to the butcher and explained that he should give us a hundred rupees for this wonderful book. The butcher set his axe down in his chopping block, looked at the book, reached into his pocket, and pulled out a hundred rupees. He gave us the *lakṣmī* and took the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

It is very hard to describe how it felt to see the causeless mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu working right in front of our eyes. We can never repay you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for letting us know Śrī Kṛṣṇa like this. We bow before you again and again and beg you to allow us to serve you on the roads of India with your most wonderful All-India Padayātrā for many years to come.

Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your servants with the All-India Padayātrā.

## Pandharpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious occasion of your appearance day, please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

For centuries in Maharashtra, Pandharpur *dhāma* has been very well known as the home of Lord Viṭṭhala, a Kṛṣṇa Deity worshiped by the saint Tukārāma Mahārāja. Pandharpur *dhāma* is located about four hundred kilometers southeast of Mumbai. Million of pilgrims from all over Maharashtra come to take *darśana* of Lord Viṭṭhala. However, since the installation of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pandharinātha three years ago, many of these pilgrims are also coming to ISKCON Pandharpur for Their *darśana*.

The most excellent display of the Maharashtrians’ devotion to Lord Viṭṭhala is the Ḍiṇḍi Yātrā, a pilgrimage on foot that culminates in Pandharpur. It has been performed annually for the last seven hundred years. This year, four of our pilgrimage groups (*ḍiṇḍis*)—one each from Pune, Aravade, Solapur, and Beed—reached ISKCON Pandharpur during the Āṣādhī Ekādaśī festival. The devotees distributed *prasādam* to more than 200,000 people, distributed lots of books, and performed *harināma*. In 1992 an article in *Back to Godhead* magazine described Pandharpur’s Āṣādhī Ekādaśī festival this way:

Every month at Pandharpur on Śukla Ekādaśī (the eleventh day of the waxing moon), a festival is held that attracts a large number of pilgrims. But, four of these festivals are especially large. And the main one, Ḍiṇḍi Yātrāthe huge Āṣādhī Ekādaśī festival—draws a crowd of 700,000 people. As many as 200,000 come on foot. The festival falls during the month of Āṣāḍha (July) and marks the beginning of Cāturmāsya, the four months of the rainy season. According to the *Padma Purāṇa,* on that day the Lord goes to sleep for four months. When He wakes up, at the end of the month of Kārttika, another festival is held, the second biggest.

At each of these festivals the devotees of ISKCON Pandharpur distribute *prasādam* to more than 100,000 people, perform *harināma,* and do book distribution.

Tukārāma Mahārāja preached throughout his life, urging everyone to take to the path of *bhakti.* His language was so simple that even the simplest villagers understood it completely. January of 2009 will see the 400th birth anniversary of Tukārāma Mahārāja. The devotees of ISKCON Pandharpur plan to use this opportunity to spread the glories of the holy names.

His Holiness Lokanāth Swami, whom you sometimes called “our Tukārāma,” has given us many instructions concerning the development of Pandharpur. He has instructed us about education, training of the devotees, organization of festivals, preaching, book distribution, and much more. Please bless us with the intelligence to achieve these goals.

In a July 1977 letter to Lokanāth Swami from Tamāl Krishna Goswami, the latter quotes you as follows:

Our Lokanath Swami is just like Saint Tukaram. The whole of India and specifically your Maharashtra are enthused with Kṛṣṇa. Now you \[Lokanāth Swami\] have to revive their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is Tukaram’s country, but now they are becoming bad politicians. So revive them by the process of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. I am very glad that you are preaching village to village and will cover all of Maharashtra.

Your servants in Pandharpur, India.

## Paris

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

After two decades of struggle, with the help and cooperation of other *yātrās,* the GBC, *sannyāsīs,* and many other devotees, as well as with the sincerity, enthusiasm, dedication, and devotion of the local devotees, the Paris *yātrā* is about to get a new temple, where Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Paris-īśvara, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi will be worshiped.

The new temple will be in Sarcelles-Saint Brice, a suburb north of Paris. The house is in a good neighborhood, near the section of downtown Paris called The Village. The area is famous for it multicultural harmony. Various religious communities—Jews, Muslims, Hindus, Christians—live in peace, and local authorities have given them facilities for their gatherings.

In the past, the city has provided a hall in this suburb where as many as eight hundred South Indians have met for their ceremonies. It seems the right time to open an ISKCON temple there. The fairly new house has ample space for the Deities, nice living quarters for the devotees, a terrace, and a garden. It is only five hundred meters from the government building. We are now working on parking facilities, which are required to get government authorization to open the temple to the public. The train station is only a ten-minute walk from the temple. The ride from Paris Gare du Nord takes twenty minutes.

We hope this temple will become the French headquarters for preaching, from which we will spread Lord Caitanya’s mission in France, inspired by your transcendental example.

I remember hearing once that a pure devotee does not do extraordinary things but that extraordinary things happen in his presence. Well, you also did extraordinary things, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but in our prayers today we ask you to let us feel your divine presence so that we’ll be able to do something extraordinary with this center. We pray to you to help us follow your teachings purely, and we ask for your benediction to help us remove all dirty things within our hearts so we can be perfect puppets in your hands.

We pray for your continued blessings and mercy.

Your servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Paris-īśvara Temple in Paris, France.  
(written by Pītavāsa Dāsa)

## Pecs

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this most auspicious day we have a great opportunity to remember you. This remembrance is our most important service. Why? Śiva teaches Pārvatī in the *Padma Purāṇa:*

*ārādhanānāṁ sarveṣāṁ viṣṇor ārādhanaṁ param*  
*tasmāt parataraṁ devi tadīyānāṁ samarcanam*

**“My dear Devī, although the** ***Vedas*** **recommend the worship of the demigods, the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is topmost. However, above the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is the rendering of service to Vaiṣṇavas, who are related to Lord Viṣṇu.”**

Among all the *tadīyās,* you are the most valuable for us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. If you had not taken the risk of carrying Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message to the West, we would have never gotten a chance to be Kṛṣṇa conscious. By strictly following your Guru Mahārāja, you gave life to millions of human beings. You gave us much more than a theory: you taught us how to put Kṛṣṇa consciousness into practice. You are a living example, a perfect *ācārya*.

By your kindness, the material miseries, namely birth, disease, old age, and death, disappear. In 1976 in Vṛndāvana a magician arrived at the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple to present his performance to you. All the devotees gathered in your room to see the amusing show. You personally took part in the performance, and sometimes the magician manifested coins from your ears, and sometimes he made them disappear. At the end of the show you smiled and asked the man: “And what about the miseries of life, namely birth, disease, old age, and death? Can you make them disappear?”

He answered humbly: “Unfortunately, I can’t”

Then you said, “But I can. I can make birth and death disappear from the lives of my disciples through teaching them this process of devotional service to the Lord. Therefore I am a greater magician than you.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best of all magicians, for you can deliver us from all anxieties by giving us the science of chanting Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s *śuddha-nāma*. We pray to be able to participate some way or other in your magical movement called ISKCON for the rest of our lives.

Your servants at Kṛṣṇaudvar, ISKCON Pecs, Hungary.

## Perth

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious occasion!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you placed your lotus feet on this part of Australia in May 1975, on the way to Melbourne to inaugurate the temple there, which you lovingly named the Mahāprabhu Mandir. During your ten-day stay in Perth, you purified this place by taking your morning walks in Hyde Park and Kings Park and on City Beach. Now when we go to these places we can remember you easily and stay focused.

After thirty-three years we have yet to find a suitable place to permanently house Their Lordships and Your Divine Grace’s *mūrti*. As you know, we have made some foolish attempts, hoping that these steps are the right ones, but somehow or other we have yet to be successful in our tiny efforts. Of course, you taught us that in Kṛṣṇa consciousness there is no loss, that every sincere effort is a victory, since we are only instruments and not the doers. Ultimately, the Supreme Lord and you know what is good for us. And therefore, you taught us, we should be patient and confident. We are confident that you are aware of our attempts to procure a place with proper zoning, etc., so that we can glorify the Lord and His pure devotee without any interruption or fear. So far our attempts have not yielded positive results. We had hoped to report the good news to you in this offering, but we could not. Now we are hoping that by the actual date of your Vyāsa-pūjā we will be able to report to you the news and celebrate the Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā festival in the new location, if you allow us to.

You gave us three main ways of preaching the Lord’s glories: book distribution, *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* and *prasādam* distribution.

We have not been able to live up to your expectations for book distribution. But with your blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are confident we can increase our efforts in that area once we are settled in a permanent place, since now most of our energy is going into finding such a place.

As for *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* we are able to go into the streets regularly, and people are becoming favorable. Of course, we can still increase a bit more, but, as always, we need your blessings.

Concerning *prasādam* distribution, somehow wherever we go people receive us very favorably and appreciate our *prasādam* very much, which shows that they are getting purified. In some places people are so much addicted to *prasādam* that they don’t eat anything but *prasādam* for the whole weekend, and some people tell us that they wait the whole year for us to participate in a particular official festival so that they can eat *prasādam* again. In some official festivals they get so disappointed because of not finding us at the event that they complain to the organizers, declaring that if they cannot get any “Hare Kṛṣṇa food” they want their money back. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said that we can conquer Australia with *prasādam,* and it looks like that’s coming true, since wherever we go people look for the *prasādam*. You also said *prasādam* is our secret weapon. So here in Perth we are making a tiny effort to please you by profusely distributing *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* and thus making people favorable to hear your message.

Please bless us so that we can rededicate our minds, words, and bodies in your service and thus become your good instruments in Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, under your captaincy.

The attendance at our own festivals is increasing steadily. So we need your blessings to cultivate the attendees nicely by clearly explaining to them the Lord’s message and making them committed to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Also, since the attendance of young people in the temple and in our programs is increasing, we need your blessings to guide them nicely they so can become part of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army.

Thank you for everything you have given us. Please keep us on the right path so that we can serve nicely.

Your servants in Perth, Australia.

## Philadelphia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

June 1974. Śrīla Prabhupāda would be arriving in the USA soon. All over America the ISKCON temples were competing for a visit from Śrīla Prabhupāda. In early February we had moved in with Lord Jagannātha to His new home on Woodlawn Avenue in the Germantown section of “Philly.” This was a big deal. It was the first *bought* temple after a series of rentals that included a west Philly row-house furnished with a grand piano in the temple room/living room; a center-city storefront in a great location that served well from May 1970 until about November 1970, when it became increasingly uninhabitable as there was no furnace; and then an old rambling stone fraternity house turned doctor’s office turned rental house in hip, decaying Germantown. But the new 1974 temple was the real deal: a mortgage, a real Deity room with beautiful French marble, a real *pūjārī* room with a stainless steel sink, a *prasādam* room sink, an old greenhouse for Tulasī, and a grape arbor yielding wine grapes that were easily transformed into grape *halavā* and grape *lāssis.* Śuddha-jīva and Trilocana had sanded all the hardwood floors and built large wooden doors for the Deity room. The new temple was “custom-readied” for the service of Their Lordships.

By June we began to assess and adjust to the reality of our temple: a very old four-bedroom, tarpaper-shingled farmhouse that at one time had been jacked up and moved from the street behind and now sat on a dirt-walled basement next to a community garden owned by nuns in an iffy, edgy neighborhood. The competition to attract a visit from Śrīla Prabhupāda was fierce. Our temple was small, with a dozen or so devotees, we weren’t among the best in book distribution, we weren’t among the best in bringing in new devotees, and we couldn’t boast anyone famous for *kīrtana*. How would we attract the pure devotee? We devised a plan: the pure devotee is attracted by Kṛṣṇa. We dressed Lord Jagannātha, Lord Balarāma, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā-devī in Their newest, most beautiful outfit and adorned Them with jewels, necklaces, and fragrant garlands made with love by His devotees. Lord Jagannātha was beautiful, and the photo showed it. The photo, along with an invitation to attend the 1974 Ratha-yātrā, was sent to Śrīla Prabhupāda, and his return letter promised us he would come the following year:

Paris 10 June, 1974  
My dear Ravindra Svarupa,  
Please accept my blessings. I am in due receipt of your letter dated June 5, and I have carefully noted the contents.  
I am glad to hear you have immediately stopped political campaigning on receipt of my letter. I have written a number of letters to the persons involved explaining how we cannot spend money and manpower on this project in competition with the big politicians. Therefore I have asked that it be stopped.  
I want to thank you a hundred times for the excellent way you are worshiping the Deity there as I can see from the color photographs you have sent. From my very childhood I was also worshiping Lord Jagannatha. When I was six years old my father gave me a ratha and I was performing the Ratha yatra in my neighborhood. And now in the western world you are worshiping Lord Jagannatha so gorgeously and it pleases me very, very much. Thank you again for the way you are conducting the deity worship in the Philadelphia temple. As for the Ratha ceremony, you go on with it, I shall attend there next year. But you go on holding a splendid ceremony for the people of the city. I am sure it will be a success.  
Your ever well-wisher,  
A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us never forget that the ISKCON Philadelphia temple is Lord Jagannātha’s home and that you came to personally see Him ride His chariot in 1975. Bless us that we can serve Him with cleanliness, punctuality, simplicity, and real affection as a king. Bless us that we remember always that we are simply assisting you in serving Him. Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us your blessings. With your blessings, and when Lord Jagannātha is ready, He will ease the way and give us the inspiration to spread His glories and mercy to all the residents of His Philadelphia.

Your sincere servants at ISKCON Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, USA.  
(written by Saudāmanī Devī Dāsī)

## Prague

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day!

To begin, I would like to offer my humble obeisances to all the Vaiṣṇava devotees of the Lord, and especially your sincere disciples, without whose mercy we, your grandchildren, would not be here. I would also like to thank all the devotees of our temple for allowing me to write the offering for you this year.

Your position is very special. You are an unalloyed devotee of the Lord and an extremely prominent *ācārya* in the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya. You are the *senapati bhakta* sent by Lord Caitanya to preach His message, and the books you wrote are the law books for humanity for the next ten thousand years.

It would be extremely offensive to think that you were just one of the charismatic leaders of a new religion that comes and goes with the times. But of course you are indeed the most charismatic leader, since your charisma is your pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness and you shine with compassion for all the conditioned souls. You do not represent a new religion but the oldest one, the *sanātana-dharma*. Your ISKCON movement is spearheading the Golden Age within Kali-yuga and grows stronger and stronger.

If you were just an ordinary person, how could you have spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world in just eleven years? When you started this movement you were elderly, in weak health, with no money or followers. Yet starting with just a handful of disciples, you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness worldwide, though you used no politics or violence to convert people.

Usually in this world, when somebody passes away his personality and message are forgotten very soon. But because you are not an ordinary person, you are not limited by you physical presence. You left behind a treasure house full of your *vāṇī,* so complete and overwhelming. With tears of ecstasy in our eyes we enjoy the traces of your *vapu,* or form, through watching your videos, reading accounts of your *līlā,* or listening to your direct disciples share with us remembrances of your nectarean presence.

Under the guidance of our respective spiritual masters, we find that you are our only gate to the spiritual world, and we desire nothing else. There is no greater source of happiness in this world than reading your books, listening to your lectures and conversations, and engaging everything in spreading your ISKCON movement. We have been wandering in this material world for millions of lifetimes, constantly burning in the intolerable forest fire of material existence, full of lusty desires. But service to you in any possible way provides the only relief, just as the moonrays soothe and cool one after a hot summer day. You are the great beneficent moon who have arisen in our lives out of your own kindness.

It is said in the scriptures that on the appearance or disappearance days of the *ācāryas* their mercy is even more available than on other days. We beg to remain exclusively your servants. Let us maintain our spiritual vows and service attitude toward our spiritual masters, along with the standards of purity in our temples. Let us maintain what was given to us and increase and spread your movement even more. Let us help you in creating many opportunities for other conditioned souls to engage in your service. There is no other happiness in this world. There is no other meaning in our otherwise completely meaningless lives.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for saving us!

Your devotees at the Śrī Śrī Nitāi Navadvīpa-candra Mandir in Prague, the Czech Republic.  
(written by Rasabihārī Devī Dāsī)

## Puerto Rico

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you and your mission!

On this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I am honored to be able to write an offering to you on behalf of your followers here in Puerto Rico. Though doing so is like offering a candle to the brilliant sun, I know my words will not go in vain.

It is said that one should not imitate the powerful but should simply follow their instructions. Please give us the strength and enthusiasm to carry out your instructions. You have given us priceless gifts. We hope that in return we will have the strength to follow your instructions and properly represent your movement.

Here in Puerto Rico the people are mostly covered by the modes of ignorance and passion, though they are always receptive to hearing about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You showed unlimited mercy and resolute determination in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village. This mission was given to you by Lord Gaurāṅga Himself, who commanded that anyone who takes birth in the land of Bhārata-varṣa is responsible for spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement to the rest of the world. You came to the West just to fulfill that instruction, as foretold by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura.

Although at present we are under the sway of the three modes, on this glorious Vyāsa-pūjā day we pray for your blessings to be able to satisfy you by following the good instructions of your bona fide representatives.

Your disciple and granddisciples at ISKCON Puerto Rico.  
(written by Navanīta-taskara Devī Dāsī)

## Pune

Respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your divine self!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, once while you were lecturing on the verse beginning *brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva,* you explained that the business of the guru is to make unfortunate souls fortunate. With every passing year, Kali is bringing increasing misfortune into everyone’s lives. Simultaneously, we are seeing with increasing clarity that you, as the preeminent guru for this age, are making the lives of millions of souls fortunate by enriching them with the grace of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

During your physical presence you glorified Kṛṣṇa ceaselessly. All day you reached out with Kṛṣṇa’s grace to whoever came to meet you, and in the wee hours of every morning you extended that same grace even to all those souls who did not or could not come to meet you—by writing your books. Thank you for your extraordinary, unparalleled example of tireless and fearless compassion.

You have kindly blessed us, your servants at Pune, in so many special ways.

1\. You told us, “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate after I am gone.” Here in Pune we are constructing a magnificent new Vedic cultural center. Hundreds of devotees, all disciples of your disciples, are working cooperatively under the guidance of your stalwart GBC representatives for Pune: His Holiness Gopāl Krishna Goswami and His Holiness Rādhānāth Swami. By the united efforts of the devotees, a large percentage of the funds for the temple construction was raised even before the construction started.

2\. One of the vital purposes of ISKCON that you formulated was to provide systematic spiritual education. By your mercy, we are doing this in a small, humble way in our current temple. Half a dozen Bhakti Shastri courses and dozens of pre-Bhakti Shastri courses are flourishing in the temple and various parts of Pune. With your mercy, we will try to offer these services on a much larger scale in the new temple, thus making it into a vibrant educational center. We seek your blessings so that the upcoming temple may broadcast far and wide your merciful message and thus serve as a lighthouse to rescue lost souls from the turbulent waters of the Age of Kali.

3\. VOICE (Vedic Oasis for Inspiration, Culture, and Education), the educational wing of ISKCON Pune, is providing specialized spiritual courses for all age groups of society, including children, teenagers, young adults, congregational devotees, and elderly Vaiṣṇavas. We are amazed, inspired, and humbled to see how, through VOICE, you are fulfilling your desire that young intelligent Indians take the practice and preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously.

4\. You repeatedly emphasized the strict morning program as the basis of all our other projects. We at Pune are witnessing the potency of this emphasis. By ensuring our rigid morning *sādhana* standards for all temple devotees, the spiritually magnetic atmosphere thus created has attracted over a hundred young educated boys to dedicate their lives to your service. It has also attracted hundreds of congregational devotees to become ever-increasingly dedicated.

Thank you for these and the innumerable other matchless gifts you have showered upon us. Although we falter at every step in following in your illustrious footsteps, your “aggressive grace” (as Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura eloquently termed the compassion of a dynamic preacher) cuts through our conditioning and enables us fallen souls to become instruments of your master plan to spread Lord Caitanya’s mercy. On your auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, we beg you to continue making us objects and instruments of your mercy.

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī Mandir, ISKCON Pune, India.

## Rāḍhadesh

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Just three decades ago, those lotus feet traversed the entire earth as you “who spoke on different subjects with thousands of meanings and who fought on thousands of battlefields and protected thousands of men” led Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army in grand style. Since then, although you have enacted your *līlā* of departure, those who constantly meditate on your divine instructions and fully endeavor to follow them are solaced by continuing to experience your presence. These great souls realize the full import of your statement “When one becomes serious to follow the mission of the spiritual master, his resolution is tantamount to seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead.” Pleased by their service, you shower such devoted souls with your blessings, and having gained such blessings, they will never exchange even a fraction of them for any benediction, material or spiritual. May we, the residents of Radhadesh, ever pray, yearn, and sincerely try to follow the example of such exalted disciples of Your Divine Grace.

The community of Radhadesh, like many of your ISKCON projects, has developed various departments as means to consistently carry on your mission: elaborate Deity worship, congregational preaching, tour program, bakery, boutique, restaurant, guesthouse, Food for Life, *harināma,* book distribution, and educational projects (such as the hosting of Bhaktivedanta College). Initiating, building up, and maintaining all this has been possible only by the inspiration and blessings of Your Divine Grace. We pray for your continued mercy so that we may see to the purity and future stability of these offerings of love. We desire that your children’s children and their descendent generations may benefit from and joyfully participate in them, in this way continuing the transcendental tradition of practicing and preaching the loving devotional service you have so mercifully bestowed upon us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, anything we know and realize about devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead is the result of your spending “gallons of blood to make \[us\] *bhāgyavān.*” More than anything or anyone else, you are for us the undeniable proof of Kṛṣṇa’s existence. Your transcendental qualities are beyond human acquisition; they are the overflowing expression of your causeless, unmotivated, and uninterrupted love for Him and qualitative oneness with Him. Who better deserves the title “the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead” than you? When we hear the ecstatic glorification of Lord Caitanya by Vāsudeva Ghoṣa—“If Lord Gaura had not appeared as the *yuga-avatāra* in this Age of Kali, then what would have become of us? How could we have tolerated living?”our hearts echo, “If Śrīla Prabhupāda had not appeared as Lord Gaura’s *senapati bhakta* in this Age of Kali, then what would have become of us? How could we have tolerated living?” You came as the messenger and representative of Lord Caitanya to awaken us and the world to the forgotten treasure house that He had opened wide when He descended with His associates. Because of you, it is possible for us to conceive of that *adhokṣaja* realm, where “all water is nectar, every word is a song, every gait is a dance, the flute is the favorite attendant, effulgence is full of transcendental bliss, and the supreme spiritual entities are all enjoyable and tasty.” Because of you, we are now privy to that which we could never have deserved, and which we cannot even desire, except by faithfully serving your instructions and committed followers.

On this holiest of days, we pray for the intelligence and purity to make your instructions one with our heart. Only thus can we dare hope that at some time in the future we will realize the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa and devotional service, by your causeless mercy.

Your servants in Radhadesh, Belgium.

## Richmond Hill/Markham

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

“A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami” and “Śrīla Prabhupāda”names so appropriate,  
as each component truly represents a wonderful trait.  
Abhay Charanaravinda—You are the servitor of the Supreme Lord’s lotus feet,  
Which are the abode of all fearlessness and hopes for the pure devotee.  
Bhaktivedanta—You know that it is devotional service to the Supreme Personality  
That is the only conclusive knowledge for all of eternity.  
Prabhupāda—You are a direct representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead,  
Imparting word for word everything Lord Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā* said.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day,  
We wish to glorify you in each and every way.  
Just one day after the celebration of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s appearance,  
You came to this earth to bless us with your presence.  
The date was Tuesday, September 1, 1896;  
“This child will become a great exponent of religion,” the astrologer predicts.  
From childhood you were a devotee—from performing *ārati* and learning *mṛdaṅga*  
To singing *bhajans* and even staging your own little Ratha-yātrā.  
Your destiny was confirmed when you first met your spiritual master in 1922—  
So impressed were you with the great Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura!  
“Spread Lord Caitanya’s message around the world—go beyond India’s border;  
If you ever get money, print and distribute books”that was his order.  
On September 17, 1965, after crossing the Atlantic via the *Jaladuta,* you did arrive;  
Our dormant feelings for Kṛṣṇa you would revive.  
You are a direct descendant in the line of the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya;  
You are our connection to Kṛṣṇa, pulling us out of the tentacles of *māyā*.  
At the age of sixty-nine, with nothing but determination and Lord Kṛṣṇa’s grace,  
You came to America fully aware of the obstacles you would face.  
For about a year you underwent a difficult struggle,  
Yet you persevered, knowing that by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy your mission would be possible.  
But because of your purity, you were able to gain followers almost immediately,  
Quickly spreading the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra indiscriminately.  
Only you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Were able to open the world’s heart to Kṛṣṇa, God.  
From Sunday Feasts to your early chanting under a tree in Tompkins Square Park,  
You attracted young Westerners on a path that would finally bring them out of the dark.  
You wrote a multitude of books, traveled the globe twelve times, and opened 108 temples,  
All with the focus of imparting *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* and the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava principles.  
In Russia, the land of atheism, in a matter of just hours you performed a spiritual miracle,  
Planting the seed in just one boy, who became your only initiated Russian disciple.  
The potency of Lord Caitanya’s message and your spiritual strength and purity  
Inspired this *bhakta* to spread your teachings to thousands in Russia single-handedly.  
There is no question that you are Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s representative;  
There is no other explanation for the worldwide Kṛṣṇa revolution you created.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, in this age, Kali-Yuga,  
You provided an effective counteraction—*bhakti-yoga*.  
You once said that among the Indians and Americans you started this movement,  
And for the next ten thousand years it will increase—this is your statement.  
By your power ISKCON continues to flourish:  
Around the world new sincere devotees come and new temples continue to be established.  
Time and time again you have shown us that you are a bona fide guru;  
By your accepting us at your lotus feet, we have become part of the fortunate few.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no way to effectively express our appreciation;  
Having opened our eyes to the truth, you are the sole cause for our salvation.  
You showed us what the real purpose of human life is and gave us an opportunity  
To take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which is the life of eternity.  
Unknowingly, we were languishing in a life of material existence,  
But you blessed us by kindly snatching us out of nescience.  
We no longer wish to play the material game;  
Our focus now is to immerse ourselves in *kṛṣṇa-prema*.  
Year after year, it is your instructions we wish to remember;  
At your lotus feet we wish to continue to surrender.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know you are with us, you are here;  
Through your books and instructions you are always near.  
You are with us through your pure *vāṇī;*  
You are with us through your bhajans glorifying Lord Hari.  
We wish to relish everything you have written and spoken;  
Your memories are what keep us awakened.  
We know that your orders can never be wrong;  
In this mission of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, please help us remain strong.  
Please continue to shower us with your unlimited mercy;  
Allow us to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa endlessly.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Gurudeva!

The humble and faithful servants of you and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara in Richmond Hill/Markham, Canada.  
(written by Mīnākṣī Devī Dāsī)

## Rijeka

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

Kṛṣṇa is so kind upon all of us lost souls of Kali-yuga that He sent you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, one of His most intimate servants, to enlighten us. Today, on this most auspicious day of your appearance, let us remember your saving grace. We, your granddisciples, although unfortunate to never have directly served you, feel strong indebtedness for the causeless mercy you showered upon us.

Spiritual life is a challenge. One has to surrender so many things, ultimately one’s independence. One has to sacrifice one’s entire being to achieve perfection in only one lifetime. But with your able guidance, thousands are doing it. How? It is a miracle. Only exceptional persons can create miracles, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the most exceptional of all.

In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.2.12 you write, “All conditioned souls are mad after these different forms of sex desire, and one shall see for himself how much he has been freed from such material hankerings based primarily on sex desire.” Even in the midst of this degraded age, where practically everybody is immersed in the ocean of such sex desires. your words of wisdom are solace to the lucky ones. By following your instructions, anybody can make it out of this material world. What an offering! To get out and go back home in this very lifetime! Who is so crazy to miss it? Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the strength to do it. Let us become your success stories. Please guide us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so we can be your true representatives. Let people say with admiration: “O, they are Prabhupādānugas!” Let us be your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Here in Rijeka you keep sending us sincere souls, although we are unable to guide them properly. Again, you have to do it. They read about your unconceivable *līlā* of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They hear your penetrating words of logic and reason, which defeat their misconceptions and leave their hearts and minds receptive to the transcendental message. Then Kṛṣṇa consciousness reappears in their hearts and feelings of happiness and love flourish. Again and again we see that your influence is not dwindling over time; in fact, it is growing stronger because you are the *ācārya* for the Golden Age of Lord Caitanya. Please accept the little practical service we can do to show you our love and appreciation for the gifts we have received from you. Ratha-yātrā, *harināma,* and book distribution are some little things we do to serve as instruments in your hands. Your *kṛpā* is in your books, as you taught us so many times. Please let us never forget this. So helpless is our position, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! We can forget everything very easily. Therefore, please let us always remember that our original position is to be particles of dust on the soles of your lotus feet.

Let us be your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
Your servants at ISKCON Rijeka, Croatia.

## Rome

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated and most respectful obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet, and all glories to your ISKCON, which is the greatest blessing for this material world!

Śrī Kṛṣṇa is inconceivable, but through your mercy we are today able to serve Him closely. We are so grateful to you.

The temple in Rome is a small preaching center, and our desire is to increase the distribution of your books. If the people read them, then what will happen is that, sooner or later, they will become devotees: there is no other way, there is no other way, there is no other way.

You know exactly what Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu really desires, and in your books you have explained it clearly, along with the process by which people will qualify to become devotees and happily surrender their lives to Lord Gaurāṅga’s mission.

Kṛṣṇa consciousness is a great school teaching the proper attitude to progress in devotional service. Attitude is a very important factor in our activities; without being humble, we will find that even the most extraordinary activity becomes useless. Actually, you taught us all what is needed to serve Kṛṣṇa with the right devotional attitude.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know our minds very well, and for that reason you strongly advised us to read, study, and distribute your books. Someone may think that your books are good cultural books but that they should be adapted to the new requirements of the modern world. But the truth is that your books are a complete and detailed map for one who wants to return, definitively and permanently, to the spiritual world and serve Śrī Kṛṣṇa lotus feet.

In Rome we want to become very serious about saving the people of the world through the distribution of your books. Just for your pleasure, we wish to give at least one book to every person who enters Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Śacīnandana’s temple. We wish to be able to turn this temple into a place where people can nourish the body with *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* and the soul with pure spiritual knowledge.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we humbly pray to Kṛṣṇa and Your Divine Grace that we may become instrumental in distributing your books and in this way receive your causeless mercy.

Your servants in Rome, Italy.  
(written by Līlānanda Dāsa)

## St. Louis

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, savior of the fallen souls, most beloved of Kṛṣṇa!

No words can really express enough appreciation for the meaning and remembrance of Kṛṣṇa you’ve so kindly and lovingly infused into our lives. We’re here trying to preserve the gift of just one of so many *yātrās* you left in your disciples’ care. Even to do this we must constantly beg for your mercy, so what do we really have to offer? On behalf of our dedicated congregation and temple devotees, we want you to know that we do love you dearly and are attempting to express that love by desiring to learn to serve you in some small way. We’re your instruments, albeit unpolished and somewhat kinked, but we nonetheless pray to be used in a way that would bring you pleasure.

Please accept the following small poetic offering:

**Praṇāmās**

We’re here again, one more year,  
Thanks to your mercy, a gift so dear.  
Every morning we try to appreciate your fame.  
You gave us the zenith—the Lord’s holy name.  
This fool was too covered to take you at first.  
And knowing this well, you gave me the thirst.  
Planted so expertly, deep in my heart,  
The will to know Kṛṣṇa did you so impart.  
Whether I was worthy or not, that transcendental seed  
Woke up by your care and answered the need.  
My heart had been vacant for want of real love,  
The kind that can only be felt from above.  
Your mercy, like the ocean, is there for us all.  
“To avail oneself” was your urgent call.  
As I try to remember from one moment to next,  
I pray that my focus becomes firmly fixed.  
I beg that your service is all that I see,  
That I act fully aware of reality.  
Śrī Kṛṣṇa Bhagavān the Lord is my goal,  
And “humble servant” my eternal role.  
Without your fearlessness, strength, and time  
I’d never have known of things sublime.  
Please let me never again forget,  
For I am now yours, ever in debt.  
Time after time, age after age,  
Let me relish your words, drink in every page.  
Your books are the medicine for now and forever.  
Your guru’s firm urging you kindly deliver.  
My only wish now is to give in return,  
But even for that, from you I must learn.  
So please carry me on with your love so complete  
Till I can bow myself down at the Lord’s lotus feet.

The servants of your servants at Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira, St. Louis, MO, USA.

## Seattle

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In the beautiful Pacific Northwest, where the sky often displays an interplay of sun and clouds and the mountains and evergreen foliage remind people of the spiritual planets, we have acted with your blessings and protection to overcome unbelievable obstacles and build a magnificent temple to glorify their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Nīla-Mādhava, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Śrī ŚrīSītā-Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa, and Hanumān.

Just one year ago our temple was but a small one-story home, lost among the opulent residences in the affluent suburb of Sammamish, Washington, near Seattle. The temple hall was only nine hundred square feet. However, on holy days like Janmāṣṭamī or Rāma-navamī one could see thousands of people making their way toward this temple, where the tireless and dedicated service of the devotees was quite evident as people were freely offered a taste of Kṛṣṇa consciousness as taught by you. In those days we believed that our dilapidated, crumbling building was not worthy of housing the Deities of the Supreme Lord of the universe, especially since we had a big enough piece of land where we could build a much bigger and suitable temple. We worried about the comfort of the Deities.

So, with help from a wonderful senior devotee who is a civil/structural engineer in another city, we started putting on paper the designs of a suitable ornate residence for our Deities. We worked tirelessly to obtain a zone change, a conditional use permit, and building permits from the city officials, environmental officials, state officials, and county officials, and we also worked to raise funds during the last many years. There was a realization that our new temple was already there. Lord Kṛṣṇa had already manifested it, and only at the suitable time would it be revealed to us. He was simply allowing us to render devotional service by being His instruments in constructing it. As stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (11.33):

*tasmāt tvam uttiṣṭha yaśo labhasva*  
*jitvāśatrūn bhuṅkṣva rājyaṁ samṛddham*  
*mayaivaite nihatāḥ pūrvam eva*  
*nimitta-mātraṁ bhava savya-sācin*

**“Therefore get up. Prepare to fight and win glory. Conquer your enemies and enjoy a flourishing kingdom. They are already put to death by My arrangement, and you, O Savyasācī, can be but an instrument in the fight.”**

The devotees working on the project kept this verse in mind and sometimes neglected their careers, businesses, and families to move the project forward. Sometimes funds were short, woefully short. However, the devotees in our congregation stuck together through adversity and gave up hard-earned funds for this purpose—funds in the thousands of dollars that sometimes were sacrificed by families with young children and competing needs. There is nothing more noble and moving than the extraordinary sacrifices of devotees who surrender to Lord Kṛṣṇa. They also worked tirelessly to cultivate new friends with genuine loving exchanges. They were the perfect examples of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s words in *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.27):

*yat karoṣi yad aśnāsi yaj juhoṣi dadāsi yat*  
*yat tapasyasi kaunteya tat kuruṣva mad-arpaṇam*

**“Whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you offer or give away, and whatever austerities you perform — do that, O son of Kuntī, as an offering to Me.”**

About a year back, after tireless efforts, many setbacks, multiple revisions, and Lord Kṛṣṇa’s unending mercy, we finally obtained the permits to build the temple. We put in an amazing amount of time and hard work to finish the architectural plans and select a building contractor; many devotees helped with fund-raising activities, as well as numerous *saṅkīrtana* parties and events. A local bank approved a loan for construction, seeing the dedication of our devotees and the progress. It was surreal to get a bank loan without having to put up a guarantee or any assets as collateral. Very soon, our Deities were moved to a beautiful temporary facility and the old building was demolished. All the work, including the demolition and moving, was done by hard-working devotees offering their services. In the spring of 2007 we organized a splendid ground-breaking ceremony in the traditional Vedic fashion, excavating a deep hole in the ground and installing Śrī Ananta Śeṣa Nāga at the construction site.

Early on, devotees realized that building a temple is not an ordinary endeavor; it requires making lot of friends with genuine love and affection. To do this we organized large-scale festivals to attract thousands of people. Many of those new people were drawn to your message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and became regular volunteers and attendees. The devotees working on these projects would spend entire evenings and weekends doing various services. Only because of your blessings and guidance were we able to organize several large-scale functions like Ratha-yātrās, a Kumbha-melā, food-distribution festivals, seniors’ appreciation dinners, Kalakshetra or art festivals, and musical performances like Anup Jalota’s *bhajana* concerts. All of us have been working hard in making these events go smoothly so we can spread the glories of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa with your genuine teachings.

At our Kumbha-melā, many volunteers worked day and night to create a replica of Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma* and organize the event. We witnessed thousands of people come and bask in the glories of Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma, kīrtana,* and *prasādam* and be entertained by numerous performers. It was proclaimed by many to be the largest Vedic cultural event the region had ever seen. Our devotees worked very hard organizing many festivals throughout last year and this year to obtain sponsorships, cook *prasādam,* set up the infrastructure, maintain accounts, publicize websites, engage in temple construction, and promote Vedic culture. In many of these events children and teachers of our Sunday school also performed many endearing recitals, dances, and plays that enthralled the audience.

Many devotees gave up their normal lives; some were tired to the point of exhaustion and breakdown. The cost of building the temple is in the millions of dollars, and raising funds was difficult. Yet during all these days we kept faith in the process shown by you and never abandoned our focus and perseverance to get a brand-new home for the Lord, for we knew the Lord was on our side and would never allow us to be unsuccessful. We thank you very much for continuously blessing us and showering unending mercy. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.5.17) says:

*tyaktvā sva-dharmaṁ caraṇāmbujaṁ harer*  
*bhajann apakvo ’tha patet tato yadi*  
*yatra kva vābhadram abhūd amuṣya kiṁ*  
*ko vārtha āpto ’bhajatāṁ sva-dharmataḥ*

**“One who has forsaken his material occupations to engage in the devotional service of the Lord may sometimes fall down while in an immature stage, yet there is no danger of his being unsuccessful. On the other hand, a nondevotee, though fully engaged in occupational duties, does not gain anything.”**

Finally, we are close to the grand opening of the new home of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Nīla-Mādhava in June 2008. The exterior is stunning and breathtaking—a sprawling, huge pink-and-peach-colored two-story building with five domes on top, with the tallest one rising sixty-three feet into the air and adorned by the auspicious Śrī Sudarśana *cakra* piercing the sky as a glorious reminder of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa. Nice gardens, parking stalls, and an outside *parikramā-mārga* surround the new temple building. Twelve huge windows pour lots of natural light into the temple hall. On the inside, a beautiful extravaganza of steel and concrete spares no details for the comfort of their Lordships. Upstairs there is a spectacular raised Deity chamber with a separate HVAC unit to control the temperature for Their Lordships. There are a huge Italian-marble-inlaid temple hall with multi-channel embedded speakers able to broadcast *kīrtanas* throughout the temple, and a *parikramā-mārga* around the Deity chamber made with donated tiles. There is a sound control room with digital equipment to enable recitals and performances in the Lord’s service, and there is a commercial kitchen with cutting-edge equipment, like commercial-grade griddle/kettle, oven, potato peelers, dough-makers, and large walk-in cooler. The huge downstairs hall has an equivalent area for serving *prasādam* and other cultural activities and events. All these facilities are there today because of your blessings. So, our temple management and devotees are simply the instruments who were blessed by the descending mercy of you and your disciples.

Thank you once again for blessing us with this brand-new facility. We will work hard to keep unity and harmony among us so that we can keep spreading your message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We will work very hard to establish excellent relationships with each other. We will work very hard to treat each devotee with the utmost respect and dignity. We will work very hard to preserve your teachings and values. We will work very hard to improve ISKCON Seattle by becoming better and more mature devotees. Please keep on blessing us perpetually so we always use our free will in unswerving devotional service, focused on spreading the glories of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa under your expert guidance and overcoming all the *anarthas* of our hearts. We end this offering with an expression of our deep gratitude to you and your wonderful disciples, who are constantly guiding us in our journey of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please forgive us for all the offenses we have committed unto your lotus feet, knowingly or unknowingly. We conclude with the following, as we consider ourselves most fortunate to have taken your shelter:

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their karma, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **19.151)**

Your humble servants at ISKCON Seattle, Washington, USA.

## Silicon Valley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the sacred dust of your feet.

We accept you as the captain of the ship to cross over the dangerous ocean of the Age of Kali because you are the perfect representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

But what was the path of Śrīla Vyāsadeva and how did he realize the ultimate fruit of the *Vedas*, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*?

Was his path easy and at once apparent?

It was not. Śrīla Vyāsadeva was despondent when he approached his guru, Nārada. And only after receiving instruction and blessings from his guru and then performing penance to achieve deep meditation did he realize and present the fruit of the *Vedas*, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Such deep realization and expert service requires uncommon flexibility and surrender that lead to a spiritual intelligence that is rare in this world. Before the Kurukṣetra battle, Arjuna recovered from the depths of despair and indecision and realized *Bhagavad-gītā* only after surrendering to Kṛṣṇa and hearing from Him. But soon after the battle he again had to resolve an impossible situation after he captured Aśvatthāmā.

Bhīma insisted that Arjuna kill Aśvatthāmā, while Draupadī implored him not to. Lord Kṛṣṇa suggested that Arjuna must satisfy both of them, leaving him to decide exactly how to do this. How could Arjuna possibly satisfy such diametrically opposed parties? Deftly he balanced the two opinions, sparing Aśvatthāmās head but cutting the jewel from his hair, effectively satisfying all parties.

In this regard, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’ve written:

Contradictory orders of different persons are impossible to carry out. Therefore a compromise was selected by Arjuna by his sharp intelligence, and he separated the jewel from the head of Aśvatthāmā. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.7.55\]

It is noteworthy that although the Supreme Lord was personally present during this dramatic event, He didn’t impose His supreme will but allowed his intimate devotees to express and resolve the conflicting opinions.

*Śāstra* speaks to us in a clear voice, but *munis* everywhere pull from it various meanings.

*Sādhus* speak the truth, but people hear their words variously and argue about their conclusions.

You told us that the truth is “simple for the simple.” But to be simple doesn’t mean to be a simpleton. A simple devotee can entertain opposing views without malice or without losing faith. You gave us volumes of instructions, in writing and in person, yet your followers disagree on many points. However, you accommodated such disagreements among your disciples, saying that they arose from “personalism.” It is natural, you said, that individuals will hold a variety of opinions.

You taught us that an advanced student can contemplate nuance and mystery—even (or, especially!) in regards to the Absolute Truth—while still finding a way to follow the thread of the spiritual master’s instructions.

This you showed by your personal example as you followed the thread of your guru’s instruction throughout your life.

At first you had planned to make money with which to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. But then Kṛṣṇa “dismantled everything,” leaving you bankrupt. (When your young audience of disciples laughed in disbelief upon hearing this about your life, you told them that it was not a laughing matter because when Kṛṣṇa takes away everything one may at first become morose.)

You also recounted how Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura had called you from within your dreams to take *sannyāsa*, and that at first you were “horrified.”

Apparently, it was only after serious contemplation and soul-searching that you took *sannyāsa*. But then you recounted how soon after you did so a bull in Delhi had “thrashed” you, leaving you with a severe injury. You said that you were alone and were wondering, “What is this?” You told us: “It was all meant for good. So don’t be afraid of tribulations.”

By your life you showed us how to face and resolve inevitable challenges and ambiguities along the spiritual path. As Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s representative, you led us to the tree of Vedic knowledge and revealed its fruit, *kṛṣṇa-nāma*, at the top.

Climbing this tree is hard work; it requires humility and courage.

Standing beneath the tree, quarreling over apparent ambiguities, requires neither.

You said, “Whatever I have to speak, I have spoken in my books. Now you try to understand it and continue your endeavor.” (Arrival Speech, 17 May 1977, Vṛndāvana)

On this auspicious day of your appearance, we, the members of ISKCON of Silicon Valley, pray to remain absorbed in reading and distributing your books, staying alert to understanding their essence by always seeking the unifying principle beyond apparent contradiction.

Your servants at ISKCON Silicon Valley, California, USA.  
(written by Vaiśeṣika Dāsa)

## Slovenia

Dear respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace and all the previous *ācāryas,* whose representative and messenger you are!

We came to ISKCON long time after you departed this world. We were attracted to the high-class knowledge you brought to the West. Never was there such an opportunity to get spiritual knowledge in such pure form. You had no interest in being recognized as a scholar, big devotee, *ācārya,* or even big leader. You saw that this world is suffering from a lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and therefore you just followed the instruction of your spiritual master, who himself was an excellent example of a pure devotee, and tried your best to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One can see a person’s greatness when the person is absent. We can see that, even more than thirty years after your departure, you are still such a great inspiration for millions. Many people are amazed by your straightforward approach and staunch refusal to give people something less then true transcendental knowledge without any tinge of material contamination. You never worried whether you would be accepted as a great saint or big devotee; you were simply concerned with satisfying Lord Kṛṣṇa. This proves that you were beyond material desire for name, fame, etc. You could have won many more followers by lowering the standard, but you didn’t. You wanted people to make a serious effort to become pure and to accept the standard of behavior taught by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself. This is very impressive, and sincere people can understand that you are presenting something special, that ISKCON is not just an another concocted spiritual movement.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, it may take ages for humanity to see what you actually have done. But we want to really understand this by Your mercy. We want to be instrumental to the success of your mission and give our best to present this spiritual knowledge, contained in your books, to as many people as we can. In this way we want to try to inspire them to accept the Kṛṣṇa conscious way of life and become perfect and happy.

All glories to you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! By your mercy may we become your sincere servants.

Your servants in New Yoga Pīṭha Dhāma, Slovenia.

## Stockholm

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on the occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day 2008. We are meditating on the first three principles of devotional service given by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī in His *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* (1.1.74). You gave them to us as *ādau gurv-āśrayam, sad-dharma-pṛcchā,* and *sādhu-mārganugamanam:* “In the beginning one should accept the shelter of a bone fide spiritual master and inquire submissively about religious principles. One should then desire to follow in the footsteps of the great devotees of the Lord.”

It is your great kindness that you brought Kṛṣṇa consciousness to us here in the West. For this immense favor we are eternally indebted to you. How can we ever thank you? Although it is not possible, we will try in our small way by reporting for your pleasure the *sevā* at the Hare Krishna Center and Govinda’s restaurant in downtown Stockholm.

ISKCON Stockholm remitted the modest sum of $3,000 in book sales to the BBT this year.

Govinda’s is Stockholm’s oldest vegetarian restaurant, with a history of thirty years of *prasādam* distribution. Lokanāth Prabhu estimates that in 2007 20,000 persons took a full meal at Govinda’s. There have been several favorable articles this year about the quality of Govinda’s fare. We dream of the day that the bars in the neighborhood and the McDonald’s around the corner will be forced to close their doors and that Govinda’s will survive and flourish. “Impossible” is a word in the fool’s dictionary, you told us.

You suggested to Ajita Prabhu that Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi be installed here in Stockholm. Since 1975 the Deity worship has been steadily continued. This year we renovated the temple room interior and offered four new outfits to Their Lordships. There is a morning and evening program daily and a Sunday Feast program, including a special class for young Vaiṣṇavas. Many new people come on Sundays and during the week, and sometimes they pay respects, inducing us to wonder who all these visitors are. For example, last fall we noticed at the temple door two middle-aged ladies and a gentleman, none of whom we had ever seen before. They told me they were followers of Walter Eidlitz in their youth and thus had a connection to your German godbrother Sadānanda Swami. I assured them that we have Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī as a common denominator, and when I returned to Gaura-Nitāi I noticed that a garland had providentially fallen at that moment on your Guru Mahārāja’s photo. I captured the incident on film (a copy of which I am offering for your pleasure.)

Three devotees took *dīkṣā* this year, and a number of aspirants in the congregation are chanting sixteen rounds daily. It appears that there is progress toward instituting the guru-disciple relationship as you intended it to be in ISKCON. Over the last year we were fortunate to have *sādhu-saṅga* with His Holiness Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami, His Holiness Kadamba Kānana Mahārāja, Ravīndra Svarūpa Prabhu, and Hari Śauri Prabhu (on his first visit to Sweden), to name a few. This year also marked the twenty-fifth anniversary of the temple’s establishment, which was celebrated by a festival attended, we are happy to report, by several old devotees whom we had not seen in recent years.

When Your Divine Grace visited Sweden in 1973, you lectured at several universities. Following in your footsteps, we had over four thousand students visit the temple this past year. They chanted the *mahā-mantra* and got *prasādam,* in addition to hearing a presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by seasoned school preachers like Bhaktinidhi Prabhu. This year our movement was also a subject of a publication by the leading Scandinavian researcher in new religious movements. When, oh when, will scholars finally distinguish you from the miscellaneous assortment of tantrics, bogus yogis, and witch doctors who mislead the innocent public in the name of religion?

A *harināma* party goes out at least once a week, often with books and *prasādam*. We had a glorious Ratha-yātrā last July on a beautiful sunny Saturday, with over two hundred devotees chanting and dancing as police escorted them through the center city.

A significant move this year was that the government approved ISKCON Stockholm’s application to formally register as a religious organization. Tapasya Prabhu, the temple president, is spokesman for the board, which manages the local assets, including the new Brahmakund property, where thirty congregational members reside. The government’s integration department also invited us to set up a book table at their annual forum last fall. With the help of the ’’BBT’ we were able to display your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* in many languages in an impressive display, which the conference participants appreciated as a colorful contribution to the variety of the convention.

The ISKCON Stockholm theater troupe, directed by Bhakta Dag, performed eight devotional dramas at festivals over the last year. *Brāhmaṇas* at the temple have also performed a number of *saṁskāra* functions on behalf of the families in our congregation.

Lastly, the boutique, managed by ParaṁgatāDāsī, has steadily contributed to the temple finances and is an attractive feature of Hare Krishna Center, selling, among other things, your books, which have recently been made available for sale on line, thanks to Govinda Nandinī Dāsī.

As a benediction, we ask that you guide us in making progress in devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa and that we may particularly learn the transcendental art of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa while striving to avoid offenses.

Your servants at ISKCON Stockholm, Sweden.  
(written by Yogīndra Dāsa)

## Sydney

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Vyāsa-pūjā always poses challenges. What tack to take this year? A poem perhaps, or should we stick to prose; I think a poem, but free-verse or formulaic? Then, should I go for deep, or just rhymingly cute; of course it must be sincere, a paean of praise, no hyperbole, a little sugary is fine, never cheesy though; pure glorification, or meaningful message? Do we reveal our heart and mind, or just stick to tried and true? Do we banalize, analyze, theorize or criticize? Should we report, “tell it as it is,” or just play it safe? Conservative or controversial, serious or sweet, subjectively me, or objectively you—decisions, decisions.

There once was a man who, following his master,  
Executed the plan—world saved from disaster.  
So the story got told of Śrī Mahāprabhu,  
With limbs of gold and love so true.  
Thus was replaced, with spiritual solution,  
The despair being traced by worldly illusion.  
Not evolution, was the message told,  
But revolution of heart and soul.  
We heard, and thought that this made sense.  
So we joined and fought—the result was immense.  
But in the Kali Age, nothing’s so easy.  
Even for a sage, things get sleazy.  
Truth and justice retreat, in hypocrisy mired;  
Revolution in defeat, and bureaucracy admired.  
Poor comment, it seems, on those we thought  
Had disciplic dreams and were Prabhupāda taught.  
Whatever the start, it’s become a doleful dirge,  
And the sadness of heart is difficult to purge.  
Yet still we hope, for what else to do?  
Save Prabhupāda’s rope, nought to cling to.  
Oh saddened rhymes, oh silent rage,  
Is it the times, or just old age?  
So many lives, and so much lost;  
Can the ideals of youth still carry the cost?  
No matter . . .  
Death does arrive, either sooner or late.  
Who then will survive in a spiritual state?  
Who will care then what is happening now  
Among little men that each other endow?  
Now come what may, please let me be blessed,  
With this prayer that I pray, the shelter of the best.

The devotees and friends of ISKCON Sydney, Australia.  
(written by Ātmārāma Dāsa)

## Taipei

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On the occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, we devotees in Taipei want to express our eternal gratitude for the wonderful gifts you have been bestowing upon us. Every day we see our lives unfold in Kṛṣṇa consciousness due to the expert guidance we have been receiving directly from you, either through your books or from your dedicated followers, who mold their lives according to these teachings. Life in this misdirected and chaotic society becomes meaningful only when we adhere to your divine instructions. Chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and following the teachings in your books constitute the only panacea and hope in this confused society.

Chinese people in Taiwan, due to ancient traditions rooted mostly in Taoism and Buddhism, tend to be indifferent to philosophies that advocate the existence of a personal God. Nevertheless, the dedicated efforts of all the devotees here are attracting more and more conditioned souls in this country to the wonderful process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness so carefully crafted by Your Divine Grace. Once they sincerely study your books and hear the holy names of the Lord, their hearts become deeply affected by this science of the soul, and gradually they become very convinced about the existence of the Absolute Truth as the Supreme Person.

It is always with great awe and happiness that we witness how the combined effects of three very powerful purifying agents—the *saṅkīrtana* of the holy names of the Lord, the Vaiṣṇava philosophy contained in your books, and Lord Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam*—eradicate all misconceptions and misgivings from the hearts of sincere souls. Purity is the only force that can infallibly pull us out of the miseries of this Age of Kali, and that purity can be achieved only by sincerely following the practice of *bhakti* given by Your Divine Grace and your sincere followers, as it has been handed down by the divine *paramparā*. Every time a new person comes to our programs, we feel your mercy manifested when we see the person captivated with genuine spiritual interest and displaying a face illumined with contentment.

Besides our regular weekend programs, we occasionally go on *harināma* processions and participate in cultural events. Book distribution by the local devotees is still very much in the budding stage, although devotees have been encouraged to join in this activity so dear to Your Divine Grace. We believe that once the Taiwanese devotees become more determined and enthusiastic in preaching our philosophy, mainly through the distribution of your books, the impact of Kṛṣṇa consciousness within this society will be felt very significantly. Taiwan has long been very open to all religious societies, and therefore we have no difficulty from the authorities here. Now we have a very urgent need to find a more dignified place to represent the Society you have created to distribute Lord Caitanya’s mercy. We sincerely pray to the Lord and seek your blessings so we may have the proper determination and resourcefulness to achieve this much-cherished goal very soon.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your continuing guidance and protection of our devotional lives. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your humble servants in Taipei, Taiwan.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-Baladeva Dāsa)

## Tallahassee

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, the shelter that delivered me from the most miserable material existence.

There is no one is this world who could have delivered me but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your mercy is your great kindness on me and all the conditioned souls of this world. Such a fool was I that I did not understand your kindness and my total lack of qualification to receive it. Now I look back and can confidently say that my choosing the path of your mercy, a path not often traveled by the conditioned souls, has made all the difference in my life. You have given me direct and easy access to the highest goal of life, though the rarity and value of this gift have gradually been revealed like a lotus flower opening to the morning light. The sacrifices you made physically to establish your mission are amazing, but your sacrifice of leaving the shelter and bliss of Vṛndāvana-dhāma is even more amazing. So I must conclude that selflessly serving the order of guru and making all sacrifices, as you did, must provide the greatest bliss and satisfaction.

Please bless me so that I may become fit and able to share you kindness with other conditioned souls, more and more. This I know will please you and bring me even closer to your lotus feet. What a most relishable and rare treat this will be! My skill and expertise in preaching are very much lacking, so I pray to you to please bless me with the knowledge and ability to be a qualified and expert preacher so that I can serve you more and more. Without your mercy, I have really nothing of value. With your mercy, I become a rare and valuable jewel in the service of Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you again and again, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at ISKCON Tallahassee, Florida, USA.  
(written by Ramīya Dāsa)

## Tokyo

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Recently I watched a new series of DVDs about Your Divine Grace produced by your wonderful disciple Yadubara Dāsa.
